**maintained the G-21 null but sharpened it from "nothing found" to a precise statement**: the
first-adopter/second-adopter contrast is a **standard, populated design** in the IFRS literature (run on
audit fees and audit time, S-265) and the field's own systematic review of ~181 top-cited IFRS-adoption
papers inventories accounting quality, comparability, liquidity, cost of capital, forecast accuracy and
foreign investment — and **post-earnings-announcement drift is not among the reviewed outcomes** (S-253) —
so the residual is an **outcome-specific unrun comparison on a standard comparator**, not an unnoticed
hole. It also added **a published conditioner that competes with the candidate's own state**: the
post-adoption information effect **flips sign** with a country-level, ex-ante, published state, linguistic
distance (S-252), which is collinear with the already-published enforcement conditioner (S-221), so the
candidate's conditioning variable must remain **firm-level** and **both** country-level conditioners
become **mandatory controls**. It also recorded **adverse premise evidence** against the
comparability-as-driver story ("accounting quality and comparability do not mediate the market effects",
S-253) **and** the supporting reading ("IFRS' mandatory nature and pre-IFRS differences compared to local
standards" — the *discontinuity* at adoption, which is the candidate's own premise), so the candidate must
now **pre-register which reading it claims**. Following the **Cycle-35 precedent** — new sources that
support the *mechanism* but not the *directional drift claim* leave the score untouched — **no re-score**:
mechanism 4 → 4, evidence 3 → 3, novelty 3 → 3, falsifiability 4 → 4, resilience 3 → 3, data 4 → 4.

**Because the re-read produced a dated, prospective route rather than another null, G-32 was OPENED** — the
**IFRS 18 adoption window** (effective for annual periods beginning on or after 1 January 2027, with a
comparability break by design: two new defined subtotals in the statement of profit or loss, mandatory
MD&A disclosure, new aggregation and disaggregation principles — S-255), read against the IFRS Foundation's
**jurisdiction profiles** (170 jurisdictions, filterable by "required for domestic public companies" versus
"permitted but not required" — S-254), with three limbs: **(a)** the calendar limb, closable now as a
*reading* task — do the published profiles identify a group A (first-time IFRS 18 adopters) and a group B
(second-time adopters) with non-overlapping, pre-registered adoption dates; **(b)** the identification limb
— does the candidate's firm-level conditioning retain identifying power *within a single standard*, i.e. is
the treatment not absorbed by the two published country-level conditioners; **(c)** the pre-adoption-claim
limb — does any 2024–2027 literature already claim the IFRS 18 drift sign (S-253 cannot answer it,
because IFRS 18 post-dates the review). **V-009 was amended, not re-created.** (ii) **G-30 (IDEA-010)
exhaustion confirmed** with **no re-probe** of statutory filings and **no crowding proxy substituted**, so
**V-010 remains non-executable** against the corporate-bond ETF universe; (iii) **G-28 (IDEA-004) remains
frozen and V-002 remains frozen**, the null recorded again, with the bounded re-check finding no published

S-241, S-242, S-243) measures a different crowd; (iv) the register's **first market-interruption family
screen** was run and logged as **R-032** (bounded negative) — venue-imposed trading halts, price bands and
market-wide circuit breakers, a family genuinely different from every family closed in R-014–R-031, with
**six** independent reasons and a dated re-screen condition, on ten metadata-verified sources
(S-256 … S-264, S-266): the effects are published and sized; the trigger state is public and real-time (so
there is no informational edge in *knowing* it); a market-wide trigger is a state common to every security,
so the trade is beta; an individual-stock halt is triggered by that stock's own news, so the identification
is inseparable from the information event; the concession is competed away inside the reopening auction, in
which the venue's own Designated Sponsors are obliged to quote; and the tradeable window is seconds wide,
in the widest-spread universe, with the published post-halt microstructure (volatility up, spreads wide,
volume up) documenting why; (v) **IDEA-014's state-free existence limb remains inconclusive** and must not

Retained pending tie resolution. **Cycle 85 record repair — the re-score moved the *resilience* dimension 3 → 2 (weight 15, −3.0); the *data* cell was mis-edited at the Cycle-16 commit (5 → 4) and the mis-edit was copied into every per-dimension breakdown from Cycle 35 on. The profile now reads 4/3/3/4/2/5 = 69.0, the recorded total was always correct, and no score, rank, tie-break, flag or gap changed; the residual documentary question is G-36**; Cycle 32: unchanged — the two Cycle-31 prior-art sources (S-188, S-189) are *partial* prior-art hits only (S-188 documents IFRS adoption **increasing** PEAD in Greece; S-189 documents mandatory adoption **reducing** private-information advantage in the UK), so the tie-break on falsifiability (4 vs 3) holds and IDEA-011 ranks first**; **Cycle 35: G-21 NARROWED to a named discriminator — the only first-adoption case in the candidate's direction (Greece, S-188) is attributed to a *disclosure shock* by its own authors; the other first-adoption case runs the opposite way (S-179), so "first adoption" does not predict the sign; the published conditioner is *enforcement* (S-221); and the mandatory-versus-voluntary comparator (S-220) implies the diffusion clock starts *before* the effective date. The residual is a first-adopter versus second-adopter drift comparison that has not been run. ## Withdrawn — IDEA-001 RECON-FLOW (76.0 at rejection) — *Cycle-13 rejection (R-011); Cycle-1 rank 1*

**Cycle-13 verdict — REJECTED.** The dossier below is preserved unaltered as the audit trail; the
decision is in `rejected_and_parked.md` (R-011) and the summary is here. Three published results
close the candidate:
1. **The effect has collapsed (S-146, *Journal of Finance* 2025).** The S&P 500 addition abnormal
   return has fallen from 7.4% in the 1990s to **less than 1% over the past decade**; deletions
   average 0.1% between 2010 and 2020; the average inclusion effect in 2020 was **−3 basis points**.
   There is no concession of tradeable size left in the modern sample — the thing the candidate
   harvests no longer exists at a measurable magnitude.
2. **The differentiator has a published null (S-146).** "There is no apparent relationship between
   net purchases [by index trackers] and the index inclusion effect, for either additions or
   deletions." The candidate's stated differentiator was that the announcement-to-effective drift
   *scales with the required passive flow*. That is now a published negative.
3. **The mechanism is published and points elsewhere (S-147, with S-151).** Speculators act as
   *anticipatory liquidity providers* around reconstitution; their pre-positioning "shifts price
   pressure away from the reconstitution date"; "apparent front-running reflects competitive
   liquidity provision and benefits index investors"; short interest **rises on implementation dates
   and falls afterwards** as speculators cover. The candidate's chain required the concession to be
   paid *between the announcement and the effective close* and to persist until the arbitrageur
   unwinds. The published structure is anticipation *before* the event and unwinding *after* it —
   and S-151 explains why nothing is left to harvest: the counterparty to a passive fund's demand is
   **the issuing firm itself**, and most demand-side institutions trade in the same direction as
   index funds, so there is no arbitrageur paying a concession to capture.
**Two gaps closed in the candidate's favour in the same cycle, which is the point of the ordering:**
G-03 (the effective-close convention is documented by the index manager itself, S-149, and by
peer-reviewed work, S-150) and G-02/G-07 (a published method for estimating benchmark-specific
tracked assets exists, S-148). The candidate therefore died on **evidence**, not on an unresolved
premise — and the two gap closures are retained as reusable research assets for the register.
Its validation specification **V-001 is withdrawn**.

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The causal chain from provider rules (S-008)
  to scheduled non-price-sensitive demand to a liquidity concession to a partial reversal is
  explicit, and each link is documented by a different body of work. Not 5 because the *differentiated*
  links (flow scaling, capacity asymmetry) are conjectures rather than theory.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (4/5):** Four independent scholarly lines
  (S-001, S-002, S-003, S-004) plus option-market evidence (S-005, S-006, S-007) and primary venue
  rules (S-008). Not 5 because the option-market evidence includes a working paper by authors
  affiliated with the index provider (S-006), and the classic samples predate today's passive scale.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** The underlying effect is forty years old and well published.
  What is claimed as new is a conditional, flow-scaled, two-leg specification. This is the lowest
  score in the register and it is deliberately visible: presenting this as a new edge would be false.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (5/5):** Event dates are
  published in advance by the index provider; the design has no look-ahead risk on the event
  schedule; the flow-size prediction is quantitative; the kill conditions are explicit.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, adverse selection, regime (3/5):** Per-event capacity
  is small by construction, the reversal leg trades against the same crowd, and the premise depends
  on a trading convention (trade at the effective close) that is not publicly guaranteed (G-03).
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (5/5):** Public announcements, public index
  weights, public prices. The one missing input — assets tracking the benchmark — is a real but
  addressable gap (G-02, G-07).

**Cycle-2 re-check.** No score change. Cycle 2 did not resolve G-02 (a benchmark-specific
passive-asset series does not exist in the public record; G-07 is opened in its place) or G-03 (the
trading convention remains undocumented; G-03 is re-framed, G-08 opened). The *general* economics
of the family were strengthened rather than the candidate: Cycle 2 established, from the futures
venue, that a scheduled passive flow buys a liquidity concession from the market (S-032, S-034),
which is the same link IDEA-001 relies on. The family-level strengthening does not change IDEA-001's
novelty score, which is capped by the age of the underlying effect.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If G-03 resolves against the candidate (index
funds no longer trade at the effective close), leg 1 disappears and the score falls toward the
mid-50s. If G-07 cannot be closed, the flow-scaling prediction becomes untestable and the score
falls further. If the effect has been arbitraged away at current passive scale, the candidate is
dead regardless of score.

**Role in the portfolio.** Strongest *first* candidate to validate (cleanest test, published
schedule, cheap falsification), weakest *new* mechanism. It is a good entry point for the pipeline
and a poor engine of discovery.

---

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 PASS on the existence limb only; no score change.** The
screen's useful result here is a *narrowing*, not a re-rating. G-13 is clean: the state the rule
conditions on is the reconstitution announcement, which publishes the event type, the weight change
and the effective date before the close the rule trades (S-029). G-12 is where the work is: the
**flow-scaling** claim conditions the *size* of a published effect and would not survive G-12 on
its own — it would be a baseline re-description with an extra regressor. What survives is the
**side asymmetry** (additions yield a harvestable concession, deletions do not — a claim about the
*existence* of a tradeable spread, where the published literature attributes the asymmetry to
investor awareness rather than flow and capacity, S-003) and the **existence of the second leg**
(a non-zero fraction of the leg-1 move reverses within 1–20 sessions, a separately dated and
separately tradeable effect). The 2/5 novelty score already prices this; the screen confirms it
and makes it precise. **Practical consequence for validation: a test that measured only the
drift's magnitude would not test what the candidate claims** — V-001 must test the asymmetry and
the reversal separately.

---

## Rank 3 — IDEA-004 SLOWCAP-COND (63.0) — *Cycle-1 rank 2; Cycle-13 rank 3, down from 74.0; Cycle 72: 66.0, rank 3, down from 69.0 at rank 2; Cycle 75: 63.0, rank 3, down from 66.0*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5, down from 5/5):** The deepest theoretical backing in the
  register: a coherent chain from equilibrium slow capital (S-017, S-018, S-019) through mandates
  and reputation (S-020, S-021) to flow-driven momentum/reversal (S-024) and synchronised exits
  (S-022, S-023), with a measurable crowding proxy already in the literature (S-026). The 5 was
  for the mechanism; the *conditioning* is separately scored below. **Cycle 13 reduces this to 4**:
  the chain is intact, but its *persistence* in the modern sample is now contradicted by published
  evidence — the momentum factor's returns fell from 0.92% to 0.16% per month after mid-2002, with
  an identified mechanism (S-156), and crowding-based factor selection has failed an out-of-sample
  test (S-172, preprint).
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (4/5):** Independent theory groups (Shin and
  co-authors; He–Xiong; Duffie; Kondor; Abreu–Brunnermeier) and independent empirics (S-025, S-026,
  S-024). Not 5 because the specific two-sided conditioning has no supporting empirical work
  located in this cycle.
- **Substantive novelty (1/5, down from 3/5 at Cycle 13 and from 2/5 at Cycle 72):** The family is textbook;
  the crowd-depth gate with a *non-monotone* conditioning was a genuinely different claim from "avoid crowded
  trades" (S-026) when the candidate was written, and it was then the most defensible novelty claim in the
  register. That claim is now **published for a different arbitrage strategy in the same asset class** (S-325);
  the **slow-capital mechanism and the flow state of the strategy complex** are published (S-326); the
  **crowding-measure inventory and an identification constraint on within-date designs** are published (S-327);
  and the *state-scaled-premium* form of the conditioning — a premium that roughly doubles when the market's
  capacity to absorb an imbalance is tight, measured on a **published, constructible security-level state** and
  identified as not mechanical reversal — is published (S-328). What remains unclaimed is a **formulation**
  difference (pairs rather than a factor strategy) and the **pair-level, netted convergence-crowd identity**
  that G-28 says cannot be constructed from published data, so the unclaimed remainder is no longer a
  *measurable* claim. **Cycle 75 note:** the score change is a novelty change only — mechanism, evidence,
  falsifiability, resilience and data are unchanged, and the candidate is **not** parked or rejected, because no
  published work shows the candidate's own effect.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  sharp — prediction 1 (non-monotonicity) is a single, decisive test — but the crowd proxy is
  lagged and noisy, and the fundamental-break confounder is not addressed by the rule.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, regime (3/5):** The hardest candidate to survive
  costs: market-neutral pairs pay twice, carry can exceed the payoff, and the left tail comes from
  the anchor failing, which the rule does not hedge.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (3/5):** Prices and the anchor are easy; the
  crowd proxy is the bottleneck (G-04).

**Cycle-13 re-check — G-13 PASS (filing-anchored), G-12 PASS on the sign limb; re-scored 74.0 →
69.0.** The score falls on **persistence**, not on mechanism, novelty or test design. Published
evidence now shows that the family whose returns the candidate harvests has decayed sharply and for
an identified reason: the momentum factor's returns dropped from 0.92% per month (January 1987 –
June 2002) to 0.16% per month afterwards, attributed to the discontinuation of positive-feedback
trading in analyst ratings after the mid-2002 Morningstar reform (S-156). This matters directly,
because IDEA-004's own dossier lists momentum as a *rival explanation* for its convergence returns
— the confounder it must separate out has itself become much weaker, and the candidate's expected
payoff shrinks with it. A 2025 preprint adds a bounded negative on the *average-return* branch of
the crowding conditioning (crowding predicts tail risk rather than average returns; crowding-based
factor selection fails out-of-sample, S-172) — preprint weight is low and its title has already
changed between versions, which is itself a warning the register has recorded elsewhere (S-152).
**The cycle's constructive output for this candidate is a proxy, not a score:** the Cycle-8 closure of
G-04 was right that no *holdings-based* timely proxy exists, but a **decision-time, public,
price-computable** crowding measure does exist — comomentum infers arbitrage activity from return
correlations (S-157) — and it has never been validated against the holdings-based measure (S-026).
That opens **G-18**; it does not license a re-specification, so V-002 stays frozen and must not be
executed until G-18 is closed.

**Cycle-2 re-check.** No score change. Cycle 2 found what a *timely, public* crowd-depth proxy
looks like — the CFTC's weekly "Index Traders" positions (S-030, S-031), which is exactly the
measure IDEA-004's conditioning wants, but in a different venue. For equities nothing equivalent
was found, so G-04 stands and the proxy-validity kill condition stands. Two indirect consequences
are recorded rather than scored: (a) IDEA-005 is now a venue in which the *same* crowd-state
question can be tested with public data, which is a research asset, not a score change; and (b)
the S-038 finding that futures-market participant positions do not predict futures risk premia is
a caution about using position data as predictors in general.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** Prediction 1 turning out monotone-and-negative
collapses the candidate into a known baseline and drops it below IDEA-003. Failure to validate the
proxy against a holdings-based crowding measure (S-026) makes it untestable as specified.

**Cycle-40 re-check — G-28 restated; no score change (69.0); the published-neighbour inventory
corrected.** Cycle 40 tested the FINRA twice-monthly short-interest route named in the Cycle-39
handoff and recorded it as a **lead with objections**: the published object is a **per-issue aggregate
across all reporting firms with no firm-level breakdown** (S-127 as extended in Cycle 40), so it
yields neither the crowd nor its concentration, and the short leg of a pair is contaminated by value,
short-bias and index strategies. Note what is *not* the objection: twice-monthly publication with an
~8-business-day lag is **faster** than the 45-day 13F grid this candidate's rule is anchored to, so a
validator must not reach for "too slow". The cycle also completed the register's inventory of the
published crowding literature — short interest is already the published measure of merger-arb shorting
and its price pressure (S-241), already a published crowding measure of arbitrage capital (S-242), and
daily short interest already infers near-real-time crowding of quantitative long-short strategies
(S-243) — which is a **correction** to this candidate's baseline, not an addition to its score. G-28
remains **open** with the unfreezing condition unchanged (a *published* measure of
convergence-crowd position concentration in the candidate's own universe, or a published validation of
a construction against an outcome); **V-002 remains frozen**.

**Cycle-72 re-read — RE-SCORED 69.0 → 66.0 (novelty 3 → 2 only), rank 2 → 3.** Cycle 72 executed the
register's prior-art re-read of this candidate — the one active candidate not re-read since Cycle 30 — against
literature it had not yet seen, and found **three** new published sources that bear on its *differentiator*
rather than on its mechanism: **S-325** (Huang, Liu, Lou & Polk 2024, *Management Science* 70(8)) publishes the
candidate's own **two-sided crowding gate** for a different arbitrage strategy in the same asset class; **S-326**
(Dong, Kang & Peress 2025, *RFS* 38(10)) publishes the **slow-capital mechanism** together with a published
flow-based state of deployed arbitrage capital (out-of-sample R² reaching 6.6 % for persistent flows, none for
transient flows); and **S-327** (Rodríguez Domínguez & Noguer i Alonso 2026, arXiv:2608.08405) publishes the
**crowding-measure inventory** and, in Proposition 3.18, an **identification constraint** — "aggregate crowding
is not identified from within-date variation by any estimator". Only **novelty** moves (3 → 2): mechanism,
evidence, falsifiability, resilience and data are unchanged. **Three new hard-stop flags** are attached (see the
ranking table). The candidate is **reclassified as an incremental variant of published crowding-conditioned
arbitrage work**. **G-28 remains OPEN and FROZEN** and its structural null is *strengthened* — the three new
crowding measures are aggregate and security- or strategy-level, so none is the pair-level, netted
convergence-crowd measure the candidate needs. **V-002 remains frozen** with a Cycle-72 note; **no gap was
opened**.

**Cycle-75 re-read — RE-SCORED 66.0 → 63.0 (novelty 2 → 1 only), rank 3 unchanged.** Cycle 75 executed a
**trigger-based** prior-art re-read of this candidate — the register's rule is that an active candidate is
re-read on a trigger (a new published neighbour, a venue-rule change, a new asset class, or a published result
that touches one of the candidate's hard-stop flags), and the trigger was **S-328** (Wang 2026,
arXiv:2605.30672, "Residual Supply and the Price of Risk Absorption"), a preprint the register had not seen.
It is the **fourth** published neighbour in the candidate's own conditional structure, and it closes the last
measurable gap in the candidate's favour: it publishes a **market-clearing model of residual-supply pricing** in
which a flow-induced imbalance is priced against the *cost of absorbing it*; it publishes a **published,
constructible state** for that cost — 13F ownership data for the thickness of the natural investor base, CRSP
liquidity variables and aggregate mutual-fund stress for absorption capacity; it publishes the **state-scaled
premium** (the premium "roughly doubles when market-wide absorption capacity is tight", concentrated in stocks
with thin investor bases and limited trading capacity); and it publishes an **identification argument** that the
pattern is "not generated" by mechanical return reversal. **Only novelty moves (2 → 1)**: mechanism (4),
evidence (4), falsifiability (3), resilience (3) and data (3) are unchanged, and the score falls 66.0 → 63.0.
The reasoning is that the candidate's *measurable* content is now published in every part: the two-sided crowding
gate (S-325), the slow-capital mechanism and the flow state (S-326), the crowding-measure inventory and the
identification constraint (S-327), and now the capacity-scaled premium with a published state and a published
identification design (S-328). What is left unclaimed is a **formulation** difference (pairs rather than a
factor strategy) and the **pair-level, netted convergence-crowd identity** — and the latter is precisely the
component **G-28 records as not constructible from published data**, so the unclaimed remainder is no longer a
measurable claim. **The candidate is not parked and not rejected**, because nothing published shows the
candidate's own effect, and the register's rule is to reject only then. **G-28 remains OPEN and FROZEN**, and
its structural null is *strengthened* by a **fifth** published measure family: S-328's absorption-capacity state
is security-level, not pair-level and netted, so it cannot represent a convergence crowd and **may not be
substituted** for the candidate's state. **V-002 remains frozen** and receives a Cycle-75 note; no
re-specification is written; **no gap was opened**; the crowding-measure literature was **not re-searched**,
because the re-read's question is a prior-art question and the register's own G-28 freeze forbids the
measure search.

**New hard-stop flags (Cycle 75).** *The candidate may not substitute a published security-level
absorption-capacity or crowding state for the pair-level, netted convergence-crowd identity (S-328, S-325,
S-326, S-327) — doing so would make the candidate a repackaging of published crowding- and capacity-scaled
premia.* *The candidate may no longer present "a premium that scales with the capacity of the capital that
arbitrages or absorbs" as unclaimed (S-328).* *Any future execution must engage the published identification
design for capacity-scaled premia and must pre-register how it separates its convergence return from the
published flow-pressure risk-absorption premium (S-328), which is a rival explanation for the same pattern.* *The
candidate must state the marginal content of the pair-netted identity over the published security-level state, or
be reclassified as a known baseline.*

**Role in the portfolio.** Strongest *new-mechanism* candidate. This is where discovery value
sits, and it is the candidate the next cycle should deepen. **Cycle 72 amendment:** the candidate was deepened
and its *discovery* content has shrunk — what remains unclaimed is a **residual** inside a published
neighbourhood, not the crowding gate or the slow-capital mechanism themselves. **Cycle 75 amendment:** the
residual has shrunk again and is now **unmeasurable rather than merely unclaimed** — the published neighbourhood
covers the gate (S-325), the mechanism (S-326), the measure inventory and the identification constraint (S-327),
and the capacity-scaled premium with a published state and a published identification design (S-328), while the
one component that is still unclaimed is the one G-28 says cannot be built. The candidate keeps rank 3 and stays
active, but it is no longer the place where the register's *discovery* content sits; that role now belongs to
IDEA-011 and IDEA-010, and the register's next marginal discovery act should be a **new mechanism family**, not
another read of SLOWCAP-COND.

---

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS (conditionally, on a filing-anchored rule); G-12 PASS; no score
change, but one open question is now closed and one design constraint is now fixed.** G-13
**resolves** the question the Cycle-5 handoff left open for this candidate: the crowd state is
built from institutional holdings filings, which are **published** documents with a **statutory
cadence** — a Form 13F must be filed within 45 days of the end of the calendar quarter and is
filed to EDGAR — so a rule anchored to a quarter boundary can use everything filed up to its
decision date. The Cycle-5 characterisation of this candidate as "quarterly-stale" was wrong about
*which* question it was blocked on: the binding constraint is **proxy validity** (G-04,
prediction 4), not timing and not publication. G-12 passes on the sign limb: the candidate's own
kill condition ("if the relation is monotone and negative throughout, the candidate collapses into
'avoid crowded trades' and should be reclassified as a known baseline") is a statement that the
conditioning **flips the sign** of the trade's expected return, which is a sign claim and not a
size claim. **Design consequence carried into validation: the decision calendar is now fixed at
filing dates**, so V-002's rebalancing windows must be re-anchored from any daily or monthly grid
to the 45-day filing cadence, and the proxy must be assembled point-in-time from filings published
up to the decision date. The data dimension stays at 3/5 because the *proxy's* validity is
unproven, not because of any timing or publication defect.

## Parked — IDEA-005 ROLLCOND (60.0) — *new in Cycle 2; parked (P-009) in Cycle 6, down from 72.0*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The chain is unusually well documented at
  *both* ends: the participant and the constraint are the regulator's own words (S-030: index
  traders replicate an index and roll "from future to future using a fixed methodology"), the
  calendar is the provider's own published rule (S-029), and the economics of a concession paid
  for supplying liquidity to a predictable trade are established (S-032, S-034). Not 5 because the
  *differentiated* link — that the concession survives only where the passive share of open
  interest is high and depth is thin — is a conjecture that no located work tests.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (2/5):** **Down from 3.** The sourcing is
  primary and independent (a regulator, a provider, and four separate peer-reviewed groups), but
  the evidence now runs against the candidate rather than merely conflicting with it: the closest
  published test of the candidate's **own state variable** — the CFTC Index Traders' share of open
  interest conditioned on commodity returns during roll periods — reports **no significant effect**
  (S-099), and the family-level work shows the aggregate effect is decayed (S-033), with
  index-trader positions not predicting near-contract excess returns (S-037, S-036). G-06 closed
  as a bounded negative finding: no located paper conditions the joint state on the concession.
  The supporting work (S-035) is a non-refereed dissertation with a sample ending in March 2010.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** **Down from 3.** The family is documented and the *unconditioned*
  trade is logged as rejected (R-004). What was claimed as new — the state, the *sign* prediction
  that follows from it, the provider-side expression, and the reconciling claim that the published
  aggregate null is a composition effect — now rests on a null rather than on an open question.
  The joint state is still unclaimed, but a claim that has never been made is not novelty; it is
  an untested proposition whose nearest partial test is negative.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** **Down from 4.**
  The dates are published years ahead and the state variable is public with a known five-day lag,
  and the cross-sectional prediction is decisive in one test. But a modern test now faces a likely
  null base effect (S-033), and the *joint* state requires contract depth — real-time observable,
  but not a published downloadable series — so the candidate cannot be tested on the same footing
  as the rest of the register without an unpublished input.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, regime (3/5):** The passive's own execution cost is
  documented at about three percent per year (S-032), which bounds what can be harvested; capacity
  per contract per roll is small; the trade consumes exactly the liquidity it prices; and the
  effect is documented to have decayed (S-033).
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** **Down from 5.** Public futures prices,
  open interest and volume; public weekly CFTC reports (S-030, S-031); a public provider
  methodology (S-029). The one non-public input is **contract depth**, which is observable in real
  time but is not a published, downloadable series.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If the cross-section is flat — that is, if the
concession does not vary with the passive share of open interest — the candidate is dead and the
published null (S-033) becomes a statement about the whole market rather than a composition effect.
If the concession is uniformly smaller than round-trip cost, the candidate is dead for a different
reason. The blocking originality check is **G-06**: the commodity basis-convergence literature has
not been read, and if the concession is a plain convergence trade with a calendar catalyst, the
candidate must be reclassified.

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 FAIL, parked (P-009).** G-13 is clean: the COT reports are
published on a weekly cadence well before the roll window, and the roll calendar is fixed by the
provider (S-029). G-12 is the failure. The candidate's own formulation is an *existence* claim —
"the concession survives only where passive share is high and depth is thin" — and on its face an
existence claim is what G-12 asks for; but the screen asks what the candidate *demonstrates*, and
the demonstration runs the other way: the closest published test of its own state variable is null
(S-099), the published work on the trader-type channel conditions convergence *speed* rather than
the nearby-to-deferred concession (S-098), and the aggregate effect the joint claim would rescue
is documented as decayed after 2012 (S-033). So the joint claim is untested, its nearest partial
test is null, and the base effect is weak in the modern sample. **It is parked rather than
rejected** — nothing published claims what it claims, and the register's rule is to reject only
when a published result *shows* the candidate's effect — and it is parked with **two** unblocking
conditions, both required: (i) a published or constructible test of the **joint** state against
the concession, and (ii) evidence that the concession survives 2012–present in the thin,
high-passive-share contracts. Until both are met it must not be presented as a candidate, must not
be given a validation specification, and must not be re-specified around a weaker proxy.
**V-004 (IDEA-005) is withdrawn.**

**Role in the portfolio.** The Cycle-2 *discovery* candidate: a new venue, a new participant, a
public state variable, and a live scientific disagreement that the candidate was designed to
resolve. It is also the register's first candidate whose *kill condition* is published by
independent researchers rather than inferred by the project. **In Cycle 6 that published kill
condition bit: the disagreement was resolved against the candidate.**

---

## Rank 2 — IDEA-003 VRP-STATE (71.0) — *Cycle-1 rank 3; Cycle-13 rank 2*

**Cycle-98 annotation — this heading is historical, and IDEA-003 is not in the active ranking.** The
register's status for IDEA-003 is "**parked (P-012) — not ranked**" and its score is **terminal**; the
heading above retains the rank the candidate held when it was last active, and it is **not** relabelled
here (no record is moved or renumbered to tidy a file). The **active ranking** is the four candidates
named in the header above — IDEA-011 (69.0, rank 1), IDEA-004 (63.0, rank 2), IDEA-009 (53.0, rank 3),
IDEA-014 (52.0, rank 4) — with IDEA-010 parked (P-014) at a terminal 55.0. **No score, rank, tie-break or
hard-stop flag changed in Cycle 98.**

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** Insurance demand → skewed premium → state
  gate is a coherent, well-anchored chain (S-016, S-014, S-012, S-013, S-015). The gate itself is
  an inference, not a theorem, which keeps it below 5.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (4/5):** Strong and independent (S-012, S-013
  from one group; S-014, S-015, S-016 from others). Not 5 because the measure of the premium
  depends on realized-variance estimation, and the "crash compensation" reading is a model-based
  interpretation with a documented rival (a variance risk factor, S-012).
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** Short variance is a known baseline. The claim is about the
  *gate*, and even that is partly anticipated by S-015. Honest score.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (4/5):** The gate is
  computable ex ante from option data only, which is a genuine methodological advantage; the
  downside is that the gate must be validated out of sample and the whole result lives in a few
  extreme events.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (3/5):** Tail concentration, margin that rises exactly
  when the position loses, and a harvest that lives in a handful of crisis events.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** Aggregate version is data-friendly;
  the single-name version and the post-2010 structural question (G-05) are not.

**Cycle-2 re-check.** No score change, and the displacement to rank 4 is caused by IDEA-005's
entry, not by any new evidence against it. G-05 is now **partially closed** (S-043): the
post-2010 change in option market structure is characterised (same-day expiries from about 5% of
SPX options volume in 2016 to 59–62% in 2025, roughly half retail, about 98% electronically
traded), and the exchange's own estimate is that net market-maker gamma is de minimis. That
*weakens* the alternative worry that the single-name channel has been distorted by 0DTE gamma,
but it does not settle whether the single-name insurance-demand channel changed size — which is
why G-05 remains open on the single-name side and the candidate keeps a 4 on data.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** The decay of the predictive power that S-013
themselves flag, and the possibility that post-2010 option market structure (0DTE) changed the
mechanism's size or sign (G-05).

---

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 PASS on the sign limb; no score change.** The state is the
live listed option surface, quoted continuously by the exchanges, and the gate is computed from the
same object the premium is measured from, so there is no publication lag, no filing calendar and
no revision — the cleanest G-13 case in the register. G-12 passes on the *sign* limb, which is
this candidate's strongest feature: the claim is not that the premium is bigger when crashes are
imminent but that its *harvestability* flips, because a state signalling an imminent crash is one
in which short-volatility exposure is uncompensated and the premium collapses. The unchanged
residuals are the tail-concentration warning and the open *literature* half of G-05 (the data
half is closed).

**Cycle-13 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 PASS on the sign limb; no score change; G-05's literature
half CLOSED against the single-name extension.** The nearest published tests of single-name variance
risk premia are unambiguous: individual equity options do **not** embed a negative variance risk
premium — single-stock variance risk premia are frequently zero or of the **opposite** sign to the
index premium, single-stock variance swap returns are insignificant, and what single-name options
price is *correlation* risk (S-153: Driessen, Maenhout & Vilkov, *JF* 2009; S-154: Carr & Wu, *RFS*
2009, where the negative premium is a large-index phenomenon). The candidate's retained *aggregate*
version is untouched by this; the single-name extension is now **foreclosed** and must not be
proposed as new. The structural context is confirmed by the regulator's own market-structure data
(0DTE at 28% of options volume in 2025, concentrated in index products and a handful of single
names; roughly 69.5% of listed equities trading options by end-2025 — S-169), which *strengthens*
the tail-concentration warning: a single-name version would have been thin even if it had been
unclaimed. Score unchanged at 71.0; the rank moves from 4 to 2 only because IDEA-001 was rejected
and IDEA-004 re-scored down.

---

## Parked — IDEA-008 SECURITY-LEND (63.0) — *new in Cycle 3; parked (P-010) in Cycle 6, down from 65.0*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The causal chain from borrow constraints and
  recall risk to forced covering and a temporary price spike is explicit, and each link is
  documented by a different body of work (S-083, S-084). Not 5 because the *differentiated* links
  (the two-legged reversal specification, the news-confound separation) are conjectures rather
  than theory, and the event is rare (~2% of loans per month in S-084).
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** S-083 (JFE, 704 citations) and S-084
  (JFE, same issue) are verified and independent; S-085 (SEC 2021) is regulator documentation.
  Not 4 or 5 because the *joint* two-legged liquidity-provision specification has no supporting
  empirical work located in this cycle, and S-084's DOI could not be independently re-derived.
- **Substantive novelty (3/5):** The short-sale-constraints family is well documented (S-083), and
  the "expensive-to-short predicts low returns" effect is the opposite direction. What is claimed
  is new: the recall-driven forced-covering spike on the *long* side, the two-legged and
  time-boxed expression, and the published state (short interest, lending fees). Modest but
  defensible novelty.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions
  are sharp (event spike, state scaling, news confound, cost floor), but the event is rare and
  the recall itself is not published — it must be inferred from fee spikes and volume anomalies.
  Not 4 because the small effective sample makes clean inference difficult.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, regime (3/5):** The covering is concentrated in
  minutes, so spread and slippage are first-order; capacity per name is small; the fundamental
  news confounder is severe (a recall driven by lost confidence is a directional loss, not a
  liquidity event).
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (2/5):** **Down from 3 in Cycle 6.** The
  candidate's own named state is not publicly published on any usable calendar: the SEC's Rule
  10c-1a loan-rate data are not publicly disseminated until **29 March 2029** at the earliest
  (S-126), the short-interest file is **twice a month** and is published roughly seven to eight
  business days after its settlement date (S-127) — so it is a stale state, not a decision-time
  one — and the recall event itself is not published and must be inferred from fee spikes and
  volume anomalies. A fundamental-news calendar is also needed.

**Cycle-3 re-check.** New candidate; no prior rank.

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 FAIL, G-12 pass in principle but moot; parked (P-010) with a dated
unblocking condition.** This is the clearest G-13 failure the screen produced, because it is not
a judgement but a calendar. The SEC's December 2025 order grants a temporary exemption "from
compliance with Rule 10c-1a **with respect to the reporting date until September 28, 2028, and with
respect to the dissemination date until March 29, 2029**" (S-126), so the earliest date on which a
borrow-cost state can be public is **29 March 2029**; the July 2025 order had set the
corresponding dates two years earlier, so the public-availability date has already been pushed
back once. The fallback state does not rescue the candidate: FINRA's own schedule shows the
short-interest file published roughly seven to eight business days after its settlement date
(S-127). G-12 would have passed — the forced-covering spike exists only when the borrow
constraint binds, so the state conditions the *existence* of the effect — but a state that cannot
be observed before the decision cannot condition a decision-time rule, and the register's
state-proxy note forbids re-specifying around a different measure. **The candidate is parked, not
rejected**, and the unblocking condition is **dated**: on or after 29 March 2029, re-read the Rule
10c-1a dissemination data and test whether a public, dated, security-level borrow-cost series
exists whose publication date precedes a decision date (gap **G-14**). The related aggregate
short-position regime (Rule 13f-2 / Form SHO) has its own dated start — "until January 2, 2028"
(S-126) — but it measures positions, not borrow cost, and must not be substituted for it.
**V-007 (IDEA-008) is withdrawn.**

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If the news confounder explains the entire
event spike (recalls driven by fundamental re-pricing, not liquidity), the candidate is dead. If
the biweekly lagged short-interest series is too stale to be a decision-time state, the candidate
fails on observability. If the effect cannot be distinguished from noise given the ~2% monthly
event rate, it is untestable as specified.

**Role in the portfolio.** The Cycle-3 *discovery* candidate in a new venue family (equities, but
the securities-lending segment rather than index events). It shares the "dated, price-insensitive
institutional flow" logic with IDEA-005 and IDEA-006 but in the short-sale venue, where the state
(borrow cost/availability) was believed to be published by exchanges and lending agents rather
than inferred. **Cycle 6 corrected that belief**: the lenders do report loan rates, but to a
regulator that is not publicly disseminating them until March 2029 (S-126), which makes the venue
the right idea on the wrong calendar.

## Withdrawn — IDEA-007 FUNDSTATE-FX (64.0) — *rejected (R-008) in Cycle 5; not a candidate*

**Cycle-3 rank change:** down from 5 to 6 (displaced by IDEA-008's entry); score unchanged at 64.0.

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The funding-liquidity channel is modelled
  and supported (S-052), and the state is published (S-054). Not 5 because the cross-sectional
  step — that dependence, not the aggregate, drives the response — is an inference from the
  mechanism, not a result.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** The Cycle-2 source closure read the
  funding-currency and global-liquidity corpus (S-052 … S-073), so the evidence base is now
  documented rather than assumed: the mechanism is supported by central-bank staff work (S-052), a
  peer-reviewed version (S-053), the CIP/hedging-demand literature (S-056, S-057, S-059) and
  official crisis documentation (S-058, S-060, S-072). Not 4 or 5 because the *cross-sectional
  claim itself* has no supporting evidence at all — everything cited supports the parent
  mechanism, not the refinement.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** **Reclassified in the Cycle-2 closure pass.** The aggregate
  funding-constraint-state → carry-return relation is published, peer-reviewed and positive
  (S-055), and the dollar-funding-liquidity → dollar-forecast relation is published in both
  working-paper and peer-reviewed form (S-052, S-053); the aggregate versions were therefore
  rejected (R-006). What is left is the **cross-sectional** structure and the publication-timing
  test — a narrow, unclaimed residual, not a discovery of the channel. 2/5 is retained, not
  raised, and it is tied for the lowest novelty in the register with IDEA-001: presenting the
  residual as if it were the channel would be false.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  clear, the publication-timing test is clean, and the data are public — but the state is
  quarterly while the market is daily, so the effective sample is small and the cross-section must
  do the work. A further constraint found in the closure pass: the *fast* version of the state
  (the cross-currency basis) is not published anywhere and must be built from market quotes.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (3/5):** Slow horizon and liquid instruments are
  favourable; the tail (the funding-contraction state is the carry-crash state) is not, and
  emerging-market currencies — the most funding-dependent — are the least liquid and the least
  two-way borrowable.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** All inputs are public and published on
  a known calendar (S-054); no proprietary data is required.

**Cycle-2 closure re-check.** G-10's verdict is **MOSTLY CLOSED**: the aggregate version of this
candidate is a published baseline (R-006), and the dossier has been narrowed (v1.1) to the
cross-sectional and publication-timing residual only. The score is unchanged at 64.0, but its
*composition* has changed: the evidence is now stronger and better sourced while the novelty is
now demonstrably confined to a narrow residual. That is a demotion in substance and a wash in
arithmetic, and the ranking should be read accordingly.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** Whether the cross-sectional structure exists at
all; whether it survives the carry-channel control (the published result claims separation, but
that claim must be re-verified in any validation); and whether the slow edge exceeds financing
and turnover costs. If the cross-section turns out to be published, the candidate is not
downgraded — it is **rejected**, and the register would hold no FX candidate at all.

**Role in the portfolio.** The register's *novelty-closure* exhibit: it demonstrates that
IDEA-002's mechanism is testable in a venue where the state is published, which is the Cycle-2
answer to the state-proxy question, and it demonstrates the closure process demoting the
register's own candidate when the literature turns out to already contain the result. It is a
candidate, but a weakly novel one with a narrow residual.

## Rank 4 — IDEA-009 PERP-FUND (42.0) — *Cycle-66 rank 4, down from 60.0; **Cycle 112: 42.0, rank 4, down from 53.0 at rank 3** — re-scored after the Cycle-109 P-5 screen returned an adverse verdict*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (2/5, down from 3/5 in Cycle 112 and from 4/5 in Cycle 66 —
  *bullet value corrected in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-66 value, and again in Cycle 112,
  which still showed the pre-Cycle-112 value*):** The causal chain from the exchange-published
  funding rate to the crowded-leverage state to the forced unwind and basis mean reversion is
  explicit, and the funding-rate mechanism is documented by the exchanges and by academic work
  (S-086, S-087). Not 5 because the *differentiated* links (the funding-rate-level reversal
  specification, the separation from momentum feedback) are conjectures rather than theory, and
  the crypto venue carries venue-specific risks. **Cycle 66 reduced this to 3** because the candidate's
  central *unconditional* sign claim is contradicted in the published evidence for one of its two
  directions (the Cycle-32 IDEA-003 precedent applies), so the chain now supports a
  **direction-conditioned** rule rather than an unconditional one.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5, up from 2/5 in Cycle 66 — *bullet value corrected
  in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-66 value*):** S-086 (arXiv:2212.06888) and S-087
  (Wharton working paper) are verified, but the literature base is thin; S-088 (SSRN) and S-089
  (Blockchain Research and Applications, 2026) are preprints or recent publications with
  author-reported results not reproduced here. Not 5 because the *joint* reversal specification
  has no supporting empirical work located in this cycle, and several sources are pending
  re-verification of identifiers. **Cycle 66 raised this to 3** because the family's evidence base is
  no longer thin — it now carries two direct tests, a systematic review, peer-reviewed microstructure
  work and the published pricing record, and **contradictory evidence is recorded rather than absent**;
  a better-grounded candidate is a higher research priority even when the evidence is unfavourable.
- **Substantive novelty (1/5, down from 2/5 in Cycle 66 and from 3/5 at inception — *bullet value corrected
  in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-66 value*):** The funding-rate mechanism and the funding-rate
  arbitrage are published (S-086, S-087), and **Cycle 13 adds two independent published works that
  cover the candidate's own claim**: perpetual-futures drawdowns quickly mean-revert while quarterly
  drawdowns persist, a higher funding rate attenuates the perpetual price's sensitivity to demand
  shocks, and the funding rate's information content is attributed to imbalanced leveraged positions
  vulnerable to subsequent unwinding (S-158); funding rates provide incremental forecasting
  information about spot volatility through exactly that channel (S-159). What remains unclaimed is
  narrower than the dossier assumed: a *dated, signed, exit-specified* rule keyed to the funding
  **level** rather than to a basis deviation — a formulation difference, not a mechanism difference,
  and the register scores it as such. **Cycle 66 takes it to the floor (2 → 1)**, because the residual
  is a formulation difference from published work in a stronger form.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (2/5, down from 3/5 in Cycle 112
  and from 4/5 in Cycle 66 — *bullet value corrected in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-66
  value, and again in Cycle 112, which still showed the pre-Cycle-112 value*):** The predictions
  are sharp (reversal, state scaling, funding-carry contribution, venue transferability, cost
  floor), the state is published with zero lag every funding period, and observations are
  frequent (every 8 hours). Not 5 because momentum feedback (past returns predict basis
  deviations with R² above 50% in S-086) is a severe confound that must be separated. **Cycle 66
  reduced this to 3** because one of the five predictions is now largely pre-answered by published
  tests and the exit rule must be re-specified conditionally — the direction-conditioned,
  volatility-conditioned form now carried by the amended V-008.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, regime (1/5, down from 2/5 in Cycle 112 — *bullet value
  corrected in Cycle 112, which still showed the pre-Cycle-112 value*):** Crypto venue risk (custody, exchange
  risk, 24/7 trading) is real and unquantified; the position's own funding cost moves with the
  same state variable that generates the signal (a self-referencing hazard); large altcoins have
  thin books; spread and slippage accumulate on frequent intraday trades.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** All inputs are published by the venue
  with zero lag (funding rates, prices, open interest, volume); no proprietary input is required.
  Venue risk is the constraint, not data availability.

**Cycle-3 re-check.** New candidate; no prior rank.

**Cycle-13 re-check — G-13 PASS (still the register's cleanest case), G-12 PASS on the sign limb;
re-scored 63.0 → 60.0 on novelty.** The candidate's screens are untouched: the funding rate is
published by the venue for each funding period, so the state has zero lag, and the *level* of the
state determines the *sign* of the position. The score falls because the family's mechanism and its
mean-reversion result are now published outside the register (S-158, S-159), leaving only a
formulation difference as the candidate's residual. The two binding warnings are unchanged: the
momentum confound (the published work shows past returns already predict basis deviations with a
high explanatory share, S-086) and crypto venue risk — and the newly published evidence sharpens
the first, because a higher funding rate is now *documented* to attenuate price sensitivity to
demand shocks, which is another way of saying that the signal may be partly a damped, mechanical
relationship rather than an exploitable reversal.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If momentum feedback explains the apparent
reversal (high funding rates coincide with upward momentum that continues), the candidate is
dead. If the effect is exchange-specific (a venue artefact rather than a mechanism), it fails
the venue-transferability test. If crypto venue risk (custody, exchange failure, funding-cadence
changes) makes the trade unexecutable at scale, the candidate fails on resilience.

**Role in the portfolio.** The Cycle-3 *venue-selection* exhibit: the funding rate is the
clearest possible instance of "the state is published by the venue", which is the Cycle-2
selection rule. It shares the "crowded-position unwind" logic with IDEA-008 (recall-driven forced
covering) but in a derivatives venue with a zero-lag published state. It also leaves the
operator's stated crypto breakout experience area while using a different mechanism (funding, not
breakout structure) — which is honest about the departure, not a return to the parked breakout
family (P-001).

---

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 PASS on the sign limb; no score change.** The funding rate is
published by the venue for each period with **zero lag**, so there is no publication-versus-
decision ordering question at all. G-12 passes on the sign limb: the candidate does not claim a
*bigger* move, it claims that the funding rate identifies which side of the book is crowded and
therefore which way the basis should revert — high funding means crowded longs and an expected
basis move *down*, low funding the reverse — so the state determines the **sign** of the position.
The published funding-rate arbitrage (S-086, S-087) is a baseline for the mechanism; the residual
is the sign-keyed reversal specification. The unchanged warnings are the momentum confound (past
returns already predict basis deviations with a high explanatory share — S-086), venue
transferability and crypto venue risk.

**Cycle-66 re-check — the candidate's own prediction now has published direct tests, and they
contradict its unconditional form; re-scored 60.0 → 53.0; reclassified as an incremental variant of
published conditional funding-reversal work; V-008 amended.** This is the register's own named
highest-value act (re-read an existing candidate against literature it has not yet seen), applied to
the one active candidate whose published-neighbour inventory was still the thinnest (S-158, S-159
only, unchanged since Cycle 13). Thirteen new sources were registered (S-310 … S-322); five were
fetched directly and text-extracted (S-315, S-316, S-319, S-320 and the venue rulebook S-321), and
the rest are Crossref-verified with index-rendered abstracts.

- **Mechanism (4 → 3).** The published evidence contradicts the candidate's central *unconditional* sign
  claim for one of its two directions. S-310 (author-reported, not peer-reviewed): across 274
  funding-rate extreme events on BTC, ETH and SOL from 2021 to 2026, events on **up days reverted by
  +1.88 % over the next 24 hours** while events on **down days continued by −1.74 %**; the +3.62 pp gap
  is reported significant (block-bootstrap 95 % CI [2.60, 4.67]) and stable around seven exogenous
  market-wide shocks (sign test p = 0.00781). S-320 (industry research, not peer-reviewed): the
  regression of the next-period price on the funding rate published at T0 gives **slope −0.668,
  R² 0.00109, F 0.388 on 357 df**, with reversals reported "in the most extreme cases". The
  Cycle-32 IDEA-003 precedent applies: a published contradiction of the central sign claim costs one
  point on mechanism. What survives is the *conditional* form, where the sign is decided by whether the
  market moved with or against the crowd — itself a decision-time observable.
- **Evidence (2 → 3).** The evidence base is no longer thin: two direct published tests of the
  candidate's own prediction (S-310, S-320), a near-null out-of-sample test of the premise (S-319:
  validation Sharpe +0.80 → holdout −1.17, "viable only with explicit regime detection"), a
  PRISMA-aligned systematic review of the family (S-312), peer-reviewed cross-venue microstructure
  work (S-313), a published venue rulebook (S-321) and the published pricing-and-clamp record
  (S-315 … S-318). **Contradictory evidence is recorded rather than absent**, and a better-grounded
  candidate is a higher research priority even where the evidence is unfavourable.
- **Novelty (2 → 1).** The residual is now a *formulation* difference from published work in a
  **stronger** form: S-310 publishes the direction-conditioned rule; S-320 publishes the unconditional
  near-null; the register's own literature-closure rule then requires the candidate to be described
  relative to both, never as a discovery.
- **Falsifiability (4 → 3).** One of the five predictions is now largely pre-answered by published
  tests, and the frozen exit rule is contradicted as to shape: the correction after a funding extreme
  is published as **realised-volatility-conditioned** (S-311: median 27.0 h high-volatility against
  3.0 h low-volatility; correction time 11.53–122.62 h), so a fixed 1–3 funding-period horizon is a
  variant, not the primary specification. The state is also **clamped and path-dependent**
  (S-315, S-317, S-318, S-321), so a percentile computed on clamped observations is a contaminated
  state.
- **Resilience (2, unchanged) and data availability (4, unchanged).** The cost floor now has a
  published fact attached to it — **spreads peak approximately two hours after standard settlement
  times** (S-313) — which is where the candidate's decision window sits; the inputs remain published by
  the venue with zero lag.
- **Hard-stop flags added.** The candidate may no longer be presented as a discovery of the
  funding-level reversal; its unconditional form is a published baseline; **a null on the unconditional
  form is not a kill**; the published tests are mandatory external benchmarks; and the state must be
  computed on clamp-excluded observations. **V-008 amended, not re-created.**
- **Key uncertainties, restated.** The candidate now fails or survives on questions that are
  *validation* questions: whether the direction-conditioned, volatility-conditioned rule survives costs
  out of sample on a venue beyond the published samples, and whether it adds anything to the published
  funding arbitrage. The momentum confound is no longer a mere rival but the **published** conditioning
  variable, so the validator must run a head-to-head test against the direction condition alone.
- **Score: 53.0, rank 4 of 5** (behind IDEA-011 and IDEA-004 at 69.0 and IDEA-010 at 68.0, ahead of
  IDEA-014 at 52.0). The ranking order is unchanged.

## Parked — IDEA-002 FUNDSTATE (63.0) — *Cycle-1 rank 4; parked (P-004)*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The chain is coherent and each link is
  well-founded (S-009, S-010, S-011); the joint link is conjectural.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** Excellent components, but no located
  work on the *combination*, and the proxy's validity is unestablished (G-01).
- **Substantive novelty (3/5):** A synthesis of three established literatures around a state
  variable that does not appear in any of them. Novel enough to be interesting, not established as
  unclaimed.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  clear; volatility is a severe confound; the proxy is the obstacle.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (3/5):** The strategy consumes exactly the liquidity it
  is trying to measure, and its own financing cost moves with the state that generates the signal.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (2/5):** The binding constraint. Order-flow data
  are obtainable; a defensible public funding-state proxy is not currently identified (G-01).

**Cycle-2 re-check.** No score change; the rank is 8 because two candidates were added above it
in Cycle 3 (IDEA-008 at 65.0 and IDEA-009 at 63.0, tied on score but not parked). IDEA-002
remains **parked** (P-004): G-01 was **narrowed, not closed**: four public funding-state
ingredients were verified (SOFR and the related GC repo rates, S-041; standing-repo-facility
take-up and the primary dealer statistics, S-042), but the *validation target* — a published,
continuous series of dealer funding value adjustments — still does not exist in the public
record, so the candidate cannot yet be tested as specified. The parking therefore stands.
**IDEA-002 and IDEA-009 are tied at 63.0; IDEA-002 is parked, IDEA-009 is retained.** The honest
ordering: an unresolved feasibility question parks a candidate even when its score matches a
retained one.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If G-01 cannot be closed, this candidate should
remain **parked, not re-specified** — re-specifying it around a weaker proxy would convert a good
idea into a disguised baseline. Cycle 2's IDEA-007 makes the reason sharper: the *same mechanism*
is testable in FX because the state is published there, which is an argument for changing venue
rather than for weakening the proxy.

## Withdrawn — IDEA-006 FIXCLOCK (59.0) — *rejected (R-009) in Cycle 5; not a candidate*

**Cycle-3 rank change:** down from 7 to 9 (displaced by IDEA-008 and IDEA-009); score unchanged at
59.0.

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The deadline chain is documented by three
  independent institutional bodies — a global regulator (S-044), the benchmark administrator
  (S-045) and the settlement utility (S-048, S-049) — and the flow's concentration is quantified
  by the FSB (over 10% of a platform's daily volume in the fix window on some days for some
  currencies). Not 5 because the link from a documented deadline to a *price* concession is now
  documented for the fix but remains an inference for the settlement-side deadlines, which are
  the candidate's residual.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** Down from 4 after the G-09 closure.
  The evidence base is now genuinely strong — five independent lines on the fix effect (S-075,
  S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), the institutional documentation (S-044, S-045, S-046, S-048,
  S-049) and a post-revision benchmark study (S-080) — but every one of them supports the
  *parent* effect, not the candidate's residual claim, and the settlement-side literature has not
  been searched at all (G-11). Not 4 or 5 for that reason.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** **Reclassified by the G-09 closure.** The headline claim — a
  price-insensitive, deadline-driven flow that moves the price at the fix, temporarily, and
  reverses — is published (S-075, S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), and the aggregate version was
  rejected (R-007). What is left is the settlement-side extension and the post-2015 persistence
  question: narrow, and not yet cleared of the literature (G-11). 2/5 ties for the lowest novelty
  in the register with IDEA-001 and IDEA-007, and it is deliberately visible.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  stated, the published fix result is available as a baseline to reproduce, and a no-macro-release
  discriminator exists; but the test requires proprietary intraday order-book data, the published
  literature's own datasets are administrator- and dealer-provided (S-074, S-076, S-080), and a
  daily-data substitute would measure a different object.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (2/5):** The weakest dimension in the register alongside
  IDEA-002: the concession is measured in pips inside a few minutes, so spread and slippage are
  first-order; the flow may already be arbitraged by the liquidity providers who are stationed
  there by construction; and the 2015 widening of the calculation window from one minute to five
  may have diluted the very effect the candidate was built on.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (3/5):** The clock is public; the flow
  measurement is not. The benchmark administrator's own tick data is not freely available (S-047,
  S-080).

**Cycle-2 closure re-check (G-09).** The verdict is **MOSTLY CLOSED**, and it went against the
candidate: the fix's price impact is published (S-075, S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), the aggregate
version was rejected (R-007), and the dossier was narrowed to v1.1. The score falls from 66.0 to
59.0 — evidence 4 → 3 and novelty 3 → 2 — because the closure found the effect published, and
because the residual it leaves behind is unsearched (G-11). The seven-point fall is the largest
single-cycle change in the register, and it is the process working as intended.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** Whether the settlement-side deadlines have any
separable price effect at all (G-11, on which the candidate is blocked — G-11 is now *partially
closed*: settlement-side mechanics are a priced friction per S-082 and S-071, but the specific
CLS deadline cluster / T+1 post-US-close window remains untested); whether the published fix
effect survived the 2015 methodology revision, which would remove the need for any new state
condition; and whether any of it is harvestable after costs by a participant who is not already
stationed at the fix. If G-11 closes against the candidate, it is rejected, not re-scored.

**Role in the portfolio.** No longer a structural candidate in its own right: it is now a *narrow
residual* attached to a published effect, blocked on a literature check. Its value in the register
is now the second demonstration of the closure process (after IDEA-007), and the register's only
candidate whose constraint is a *funding* deadline rather than a price-setting event — the part of
the clock that no fix paper covers.

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The funding-liquidity channel is modelled
  and supported (S-052), and the state is published (S-054). Not 5 because the cross-sectional
  step — that dependence, not the aggregate, drives the response — is an inference from the
  mechanism, not a result.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** The Cycle-2 source closure read the
  funding-currency and global-liquidity corpus (S-052 … S-073), so the evidence base is now
  documented rather than assumed: the mechanism is supported by central-bank staff work (S-052), a
  peer-reviewed version (S-053), the CIP/hedging-demand literature (S-056, S-057, S-059) and
  official crisis documentation (S-058, S-060, S-072). Not 4 or 5 because the *cross-sectional
  claim itself* has no supporting evidence at all — everything cited supports the parent
  mechanism, not the refinement.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** **Reclassified in the Cycle-2 closure pass.** The aggregate
  funding-constraint-state → carry-return relation is published, peer-reviewed and positive
  (S-055), and the dollar-funding-liquidity → dollar-forecast relation is published in both
  working-paper and peer-reviewed form (S-052, S-053); the aggregate versions were therefore
  rejected (R-006). What is left is the **cross-sectional** structure and the publication-timing
  test — a narrow, unclaimed residual, not a discovery of the channel. 2/5 is retained, not
  raised, and it is tied for the lowest novelty in the register with IDEA-001: presenting the
  residual as if it were the channel would be false.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  clear, the publication-timing test is clean, and the data are public — but the state is
  quarterly while the market is daily, so the effective sample is small and the cross-section must
  do the work. A further constraint found in the closure pass: the *fast* version of the state
  (the cross-currency basis) is not published anywhere and must be built from market quotes.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (3/5):** Slow horizon and liquid instruments are
  favourable; the tail (the funding-contraction state is the carry-crash state) is not, and
  emerging-market currencies — the most funding-dependent — are the least liquid and the least
  two-way borrowable.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** All inputs are public and published on
  a known calendar (S-054); no proprietary data is required.

**Cycle-2 closure re-check.** G-10's verdict is **MOSTLY CLOSED**: the aggregate version of this
candidate is a published baseline (R-006), and the dossier has been narrowed (v1.1) to the
cross-sectional and publication-timing residual only. The score is unchanged at 64.0, but its
*composition* has changed: the evidence is now stronger and better sourced while the novelty is
now demonstrably confined to a narrow residual. That is a demotion in substance and a wash in
arithmetic, and the ranking should be read accordingly.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** Whether the cross-sectional structure exists at
all; whether it survives the carry-channel control (the published result claims separation, but
that claim must be re-verified in any validation); and whether the slow edge exceeds financing
and turnover costs. If the cross-section turns out to be published, the candidate is not
downgraded — it is **rejected**, and the register would hold no FX candidate at all.

**Role in the portfolio.** The register's *novelty-closure* exhibit: it demonstrates that
IDEA-002's mechanism is testable in a venue where the state is published, which is the Cycle-2
answer to the state-proxy question, and it demonstrates the closure process demoting the
register's own candidate when the literature turns out to already contain the result. It is a
candidate, but a weakly novel one with a narrow residual.

## Withdrawn — IDEA-006 FIXCLOCK (59.0) — *Cycle-4 re-check; rejected (R-009) in Cycle 5*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The deadline chain is documented by three
  independent institutional bodies — a global regulator (S-044), the benchmark administrator
  (S-045) and the settlement utility (S-048, S-049) — and the flow's concentration is quantified
  by the FSB (over 10% of a platform's daily volume in the fix window on some days for some
  currencies). Not 5 because the link from a documented deadline to a *price* concession is now
  documented for the fix but remains an inference for the settlement-side deadlines, which are
  the candidate's residual.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** Down from 4 after the G-09 closure.
  The evidence base is now genuinely strong — five independent lines on the fix effect (S-075,
  S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), the institutional documentation (S-044, S-045, S-046, S-048,
  S-049) and a post-revision benchmark study (S-080) — but every one of them supports the
  *parent* effect, not the candidate's residual claim, and the settlement-side literature has not
  been searched at all (G-11). Not 4 or 5 for that reason.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** **Reclassified by the G-09 closure.** The headline claim — a
  price-insensitive, deadline-driven flow that moves the price at the fix, temporarily, and
  reverses — is published (S-075, S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), and the aggregate version was
  rejected (R-007). What is left is the settlement-side extension and the post-2015 persistence
  question: narrow, and not yet cleared of the literature (G-11). 2/5 ties for the lowest novelty
  in the register with IDEA-001 and IDEA-007, and it is deliberately visible.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  stated, the published fix result is available as a baseline to reproduce, and a no-macro-release
  discriminator exists; but the test requires proprietary intraday order-book data, the published
  literature's own datasets are administrator- and dealer-provided (S-074, S-076, S-080), and a
  daily-data substitute would measure a different object.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (2/5):** The weakest dimension in the register alongside
  IDEA-002: the concession is measured in pips inside a few minutes, so spread and slippage are
  first-order; the flow may already be arbitraged by the liquidity providers who are stationed
  there by construction; and the 2015 widening of the calculation window from one minute to five
  may have diluted the very effect the candidate was built on.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (3/5):** The clock is public; the flow
  measurement is not. The benchmark administrator's own tick data is not freely available (S-047,
  S-080).

**Cycle-2 closure re-check (G-09).** The verdict is **MOSTLY CLOSED**, and it went against the
candidate: the fix's price impact is published (S-075, S-076, S-077, S-074, S-081), the aggregate
version was rejected (R-007), and the dossier was narrowed to v1.1. The score falls from 66.0 to
59.0 — evidence 4 → 3 and novelty 3 → 2 — because the closure found the effect published, and
because the residual it leaves behind is unsearched (G-11). The seven-point fall is the largest
single-cycle change in the register, and it is the process working as intended.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** Whether the settlement-side deadlines have any
separable price effect at all (G-11, on which the candidate is blocked); whether the published fix
effect survived the 2015 methodology revision, which would remove the need for any new state
condition; and whether any of it is harvestable after costs by a participant who is not already
stationed at the fix. If G-11 closes against the candidate, it is rejected, not re-scored.

**Role in the portfolio.** No longer a structural candidate in its own right: it is now a *narrow
residual* attached to a published effect, blocked on a literature check. Its value in the register
is now the second demonstration of the closure process (after IDEA-007), and the register's only
candidate whose constraint is a *funding* deadline rather than a price-setting event — the part of
the clock that no fix paper covers.
