## Parked — IDEA-010 CREDIT-ETF (55.0) — *new in Cycle 4; Cycle-13 rank 4; Cycle 32: 68.0, rank 2, up from 65.0; heading corrected in Cycle 75, which had still shown the Cycle-13 score; **parked (P-014) in Cycle 78, down from 68.0** — unranked*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The AP balance-sheet constraint channel is
  documented (Pan & Zeng 2019; Eisfeldt et al. 2024), and the state (ETF flow, AP stress proxies)
  is published with known lags. Not 5 because the *joint* state-conditioned two-legged
  specification is conjectural, and AP-level balance-sheet data is commercial rather than public.
  **Unchanged in Cycle 78** — the mechanism is published and the parking decision does not alter it.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (4/5, up from 3/5 in Cycle 32 — *bullet value
  corrected in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-32 value*):** the state-to-premium relation is
  published on a **filing-based state** — Raddatz (S-194) measures APs' regulatory capital ratios from SEC
  regulatory filings and shows that they, and not other AP characteristics, govern ETF arbitrage intensity
  and premia (0.69pp versus 0.39pp); the mechanism is published (S-195, **now line-verified**); the AP-capital
  channel is quantified again in S-323 (a one-standard-deviation deterioration in active AP balance sheets
  raises ETF–NAV differentials by nearly 10 % of a standard deviation); and the published rivals stand at
  five — S-196 (fractional baskets), S-197 (funding segmentation), S-199 (client liquidity discounts),
  **S-331 (the intermediary-attenuation account)** and **S-195's own volatility / illiquidity / trading-cost
  conditioner**. Not 5 because the candidate's **own** two-legged specification (AP stress × ETF flow →
  ETF–NAV discount → mean reversion) still has no supporting empirical work located: the evidence is a
  *family* and a set of rivals, not the candidate's rule. **Unchanged in Cycle 78**, even though four of
  the five rivals were **line-verified in Cycle 78** (S-196, S-197, S-199 and, in the same pass, S-200):
  verification raises confidence in the *family*, which the Cycle-35/52 precedent says does not move the
  evidence dimension, and it does not produce the candidate's own relation.
- **Substantive novelty (1/5, down from 2/5 in Cycle 6 and from 3/5 at inception — *bullet value corrected
  in Cycle 76, which still showed the Cycle-6 value*):** **Down from 3 in Cycle 6.** The ETF arbitrage-limits family is
  documented, and — decisively for this candidate — the **mechanism's core is published
  institutionally**: the FCA's own study documents the connection between ETF mispricing and AP
  inventory (S-123), using a regulator's MiFID II database to see AP inventory at all, and that
  study is already in the register's baseline table. So the candidate's residual is the *joint
  state plus a trade specification*, not a new mechanism, and a novelty score of 3 was implicitly
  denying the published baseline. **Cycle 32 takes it to the floor (2 → 1):** the state-to-premium relation
  is **published** (S-194, with the mechanism in S-195), so the candidate is a **trade specification and
  cost-floor test, never a discovery of the relation**. Cycle 76 adds two facts at that floor — the same
  relation has been published with a **second state variable** (AP leverage, in the 2021 working-paper
  version of S-194) and with a **published competitor conditioner** (S-195) — but publishes **nothing that
  shows the candidate's own effect**, so the candidate is **not** parked and **not** rejected.
  **Cycle 78 — the candidate IS parked, and the novelty floor is unchanged by that.** The parking rests on
  the *measurability* of the residual (G-34), not on any new publication of the relation: nothing in
  Cycle 78 shows the candidate's own effect either, which is why the candidate is parked rather than
  rejected.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (2/5, down from 4/5 — *Cycle-78
  change, at parking*):** The predictions are sharp (discount widens under AP stress + negative flow,
  mean-reverts, absent when capacity ample, cost floor) and the kill conditions are explicit, so the
  *clarity* half of this dimension survives. The *feasibility of a clean test* half does not: the state
  cannot be attached to a corporate-bond ETF from public filings (G-29: 0 corporate-bond ETFs with a
  published AP set; G-30 exhausted across all four outside routes), and **no published discriminator
  separates the transient reading of the ETF–NAV widening from the mechanical one** (G-34's kill condition
  fired in Cycle 77). The candidate therefore cannot be tested as specified — the IDEA-014 precedent (a
  state failure costs falsifiability points), applied to a state failure of the stronger kind.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (2/5, down from 3/5 — *Cycle-78 change, at parking*):** Corporate
  bond ETFs and their underlying bonds are less liquid than equities; the spread is crossed twice; the
  position is long illiquid bonds into a potentially falling market during stress. Capacity is bounded by
  the ETF flow and the underlying bond liquidity. **Cycle 78 lowers this reading** because the Cycle-76
  offset that left it unmoved — a named discriminator existed to pre-register — is gone: the discriminator
  is **unpublished**, so the mechanical reading (S-196, now line-verified: "ETFs with fractional baskets
  exhibit persistent premiums and discounts"; S-331) cannot be excluded in advance, and the published
  sign-reversal mechanism (S-195) stands as a live failure mode alongside the cost-overwhelm condition.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (2/5, down from 4/5 — *Cycle-78 change, at parking*;
  up from 3/5 in Cycle 32 — *bullet value corrected in Cycle 76, which still showed the Cycle-6 value*):**
  TRACE bond data and ETF prices/NAV are public; creation/redemption baskets are published daily by the
  sponsor; and the **state construct is publicly reconstructable** from regulatory filings — Form X-17A-5
  for private broker-dealers (S-231), Exchange Act periodic reports for listed broker-dealers (S-233) and
  FR Y-9C for bank holding companies (S-226) — which is why the dimension was raised in Cycle 32 when G-16
  closed in the candidate's favour. **Cycle 78 lowers it to 2** because that Cycle-32 rationale is **false for
  the candidate's own universe**: the capital state is public in principle, but the **ETF → AP mapping is
  not attachable** for any corporate-bond ETF (G-29 coverage 0; G-30 exhausted), so the candidate
  **cannot be constructed at all**, and the published substitutes (SRF take-up, TRACE dealer and interdealer
  measures) are *different constructs* that have not been validated against any dealer-balance-sheet
  measure. The register's prohibition on substituting them stands.

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PARTIAL PASS, G-12 PASS on the existence limb; re-scored 68.0 → 65.0
(novelty 3 → 2).** The *flow* leg is clean — ETF shares outstanding and the creation/redemption
baskets are published daily by the sponsor — but the *differentiating* leg, AP balance-sheet
stress, is not published in the form the dossier specifies; the dossier's own Observables line
substitutes proxies for the AP state (dealer positions, interdealer price dispersion, SRF
take-up), and those proxies are computable from published data but are different constructs that
have not been validated. Under the register's own rule, re-specifying around an unvalidated proxy
is not permitted, so the candidate is retained **only** with an explicit precondition: **G-16** —
validate the published proxy set against a dealer-balance-sheet measure before any specification
is written. G-12 passes on the existence limb (prediction 3: "the effect is absent when AP
capacity is ample"), but the mechanism it conditions is published (S-123), which is what the
novelty re-score records. **No validation specification is to be written for IDEA-010 until G-16
is closed.**

**Cycle-40 re-check — G-29 executed as a calibrated document-level search; G-27 closed at filer
level; no score change (68.0).** The register's trade-press lead was verified from the primary
document (S-239: the iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF Form 424B3 prospectuses name their APs verbatim — four
cash APs in January 2024, twelve cash and four in-kind as of 31 July 2025), which gave Cycle 40 a
detector known to work. Run across every prospectus form type, that detector returns **five
documents in four filers, all crypto or commodity trusts, and no corporate-bond ETF**; the
complementary phrase-first (591 bond-worded filings) and name-first (thirty-one AP firm names) routes
give the same answer. **Coverage: 0 corporate-bond ETFs with a published AP set; 0 (ETF, AP) cells; 0
(ETF, AP, episode) cells** — so the state **cannot be attached to a corporate-bond ETF from public
filings**: *untested, not dead*, and no published crowding proxy may be substituted. The G-27 counts
were re-derived at filer level (2,419 distinct X-17A-5 filers as a lower bound; 66 listed filers
quarterly and 96 annual; FR Y-9C not indexed by EDGAR at all), and the private-AP capital state is
**annual** — 9–14 months stale, a correction to the Cycle-37 cadence statement in S-231, which
*reinforces* the cross-sectional re-anchoring because the state never changes inside a holding
window. New gap **G-30**: is there a published route *outside* the statutory prospectus to a
corporate-bond ETF's AP set.

**Cycle-76 re-check — the trigger-based prior-art re-read Cycle 75 handed on was executed; no score change
(68.0), rank 2 unchanged; two new hard-stop flags; gap G-34 opened.** **S-331 was line-verified** (author-
hosted PDF fetched directly, HTTP 200, 978,520 bytes, 63 pages, text extracted with `pypdf` — the same method
already used for S-323 in Cycle 75; the Cycle-75 failure was a **tooling gap, not a document defect**), which
removed the register's stated precondition, and the re-read was run. The published result is a **neighbour of
the candidate's mechanism, not the candidate's own relation**: it establishes that "the price impact of
investor demand depends critically on institutional structure" and that a flow executed by a specialised
intermediary with spare capacity has "statistically insignificant and economically negligible price impact"
— which supports the candidate's *existence premise in general form* and simultaneously supplies a **rival
explanation** for the candidate's effect (the ETF–NAV widening may be a mechanical consequence of
intermediated creation/redemption pricing rather than a transient dislocation a liquidity supplier can
monetise). A **verification pass over the candidate's load-bearing neighbours** upgraded **S-195** to partly
line-verified and produced two further published findings: a **competitor conditioner** of the same relation
(market volatility, bond-market illiquidity, trading costs — available at decision time where the candidate's
own state is not) and a **sign-reversal mechanism** ("ETF arbitrage may go in the opposite direction than what
would be implied by the initial relative mispricing"). **New hard-stop flags:** (i) the candidate may not
present the ETF–NAV widening as a price-pressure effect without pre-registering a discriminator against the
published intermediary-attenuation account (S-331) and the published mechanical-pricing account (S-196);
(ii) the candidate may not be presented as uniquely state-conditioned, because a published competitor
conditioner of the same relation is reachable where the candidate's own state is not. **G-34 opened** with a
kill condition (no published transient/mechanical decomposition and no decision-time discriminating state ⇒
the residual is **unmeasurable as specified** and the candidate must be re-classified, not re-specified);
G-34 does **not** unblock V-010, which remains non-executable, and G-30 was **not** re-probed. No `S-`, `V-`
or `R-` identifier was consumed.

**Cycle-78 re-check — the G-34 kill condition fires and the candidate is PARKED (P-014), terminal score 55.0.**
The Cycle-77 bounded literature operation returned **nothing on either limb of G-34**: no published work
decomposes a corporate-bond ETF's ETF–NAV discount into a **transient** and a **mechanical** component,
and none identifies a **published, decision-time** state that discriminates between them in that universe
without the ETF→AP mapping. The pre-registered rule from Cycle 76 fires: the residual is **unmeasurable as
specified**, and the candidate is **re-classified, not re-specified**. The register chose **park over
downgrade**, on three stated grounds: (i) the state is **not attachable** to the candidate's own universe
(G-29 coverage 0, G-30 exhausted — the IDEA-002 case); (ii) the measurable content is **published**
(S-194, S-195, S-323 — the IDEA-003 case); and (iii) the **rejection** ground is not met, because the
register rejects only when a published result **shows the candidate's own effect**, and none does — so the
candidate is parked, **not rejected**. **Score: 68.0 → 55.0, terminal and unranked** (mechanism 4, evidence
4, novelty 1, falsifiability 2, resilience 2, data 2 — each of the three moving dimensions moving for its own
already-established reason, not for the kill condition alone). **V-010 is marked never-to-execute.** The
cycle's second operation was a **verification pass over IDEA-010's published neighbours** that upgraded
**five** index-rendered entries in place — **S-196, S-197, S-198, S-199, S-200** — and corrected two
dates (S-196: BIS WP 975 is **November 2021**, not June 2022; S-199: BoE Staff WP **1126, May 2025**, not
April 2025). Two of the upgraded documents bear directly on the parking: **S-196** states that "**ETFs with
fractional baskets exhibit persistent premiums and discounts**", and **S-198** publishes the
AP-balance-sheet account in the same universe (SMCCF purchases produced intraday spillovers and next-day
inflows "at a price premium to NAV") — the **mechanical** account of the candidate's outcome is published in
the candidate's own universe, and no published state separates it from the transient one. That is written to
the known-baseline table as **row 84**. No `S-`, `V-`, `R-` or `IDEA-` identifier was consumed; **G-35** was
registered and closed.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank — now re-admission conditions, not rank conditions (the
candidate is parked and unranked).** If AP-level stress proxies cannot be validated against disclosed
dealer balance sheets, the candidate cannot be tested as specified and should not be re-specified on a
weaker proxy. If the ETF–NAV discount does not mean-revert within 1–10 sessions after costs, the candidate
is dead. If fundamental bond re-pricing during stress explains the entire discount move, the candidate
collapses into a known baseline. **The first of these has already fired** (G-29/G-30) and the second is
unreachable while the first stands, which is the substance of the parking. The full re-admission condition
is written in `rejected_and_parked.md` under **P-014**.

**Role in the portfolio.** A corporate-credit instantiation of the "dated, price-insensitive
institutional flow buys a liquidity concession" template (shared with IDEA-005 and IDEA-006), in
a venue where the state (ETF flow) is published daily but the AP-level constraint is commercial.
