#### Cycle 112 block — IDEA-009 · the Cycle-109 P-5 screen returned an adverse verdict

- **What the screen asked.** The Cycle-109 P-5 audit named the only two findings that would change this
  candidate's verdict: **(A)** a published test of the *unconditional* funding-level reversal on a venue
  other than Binance or BitMEX (the venues of S-310, S-320); **(B)** a published result that the
  *conditional* form's edge is absent or fails out of sample. Both were pre-registered before the first
  fetch, with the falsification rule fixed in advance: a new source moves a score only if it bears on the
  **sign** claim or on **out-of-sample failure**.
- **What was found.** **(A) is not satisfied** — no published test of the unconditional form on a named
  third venue was located (S-390 is Binance; the portion of S-389 read does not identify its three venues).
  **(B) is not satisfied** in the strict form either. But the adjacent evidence is adverse and is recorded as
  adverse, not as a refutation: **S-390** (Memon, Anklesaria-Dalal & Mishra 2026, fetched directly) is an
  independent test of the candidate's *own* trailing-percentile construction — "the current funding rate's
  mid-rank percentile within the preceding 180-day distribution", Binance BTCUSDT, 10 September 2019 to
  10 August 2026, controlled for the four-hour pre-settlement return — whose result is **asymmetric**
  ("extreme negative funding is followed by an average 24-hour Bitcoin return of 0.506 % … **Extreme positive
  funding does not exhibit a comparable 24-hour effect**"); **S-389** (a line-verified 791-contract
  practitioner note) reports the raw level's next-24-hour information coefficient flipping from **−3.9 %
  (2020)** to **+3.4 % (2025, t = 8.1)** and **+2.0 % (2026, t = 3.6)** — "No unconditional level signal
  survives this flip" — reports the candidate's percentile construction as **null** (z-score 24-hour
  information coefficient **−0.0007, t = −0.3**), **contradicts the crowd-unwind link** of the mechanism
  ("open interest builds into and through high-funding settlements … positions are opened into the payment, not
  unwound before it"), and puts the effect's excess over the round trip at "**+2.2 bps, t = 0.68**"; **S-391**
  is the **rediscovery guard** — the venue published the same rule in **2017**, with per-cell success rates of
  47.62 %, 53.23 %, 45.68 % and 44.44 % over 524 funding periods; **S-392** supplies the cost and decay
  anchors (13 bps per round trip; 100–135 hours to repay; the spread 20–35 % smaller in the second year; and
  "OKX publishes only three months of history", which bears on the venue-transferability condition).
- **Scores: 53.0 → 42.0.** Mechanism **3 → 2** (the crowd link and the direction link are both contradicted;
  the transfer caveat is recorded — S-389 is cross-sectional and the candidate's rule is time-series within
  one underlying, so the regime finding is suggestive rather than decisive, while the "positions are opened
  into the payment" finding is a statement about what the state measures and does transfer); falsifiability
  **3 → 2** (three of five predictions are now largely pre-answered — the Cycle-66 precedent); resilience
  **2 → 1** (the sign is inverted in the current regime rather than merely weaker, and capacity binds);
  evidence **3 → 3** (the base is no longer thin and contradictory evidence is recorded rather than absent,
  but the new material is two practitioner notes, a fee-charging journal article and an interested party's
  blog — volume rose, standing did not); novelty **1 → 1** (at the floor, and the floor is now documented
  from the venue's own mouth); data **4 → 4** (unchanged).
- **Two new hard-stop conditions:** *costs likely to overwhelm the hypothesized effect*; *a claim that merely
  repackages a known idea*. The **momentum-confound condition** is sharpened — the funding signal's sign
  tracks the prevailing regime — and the **crypto venue risk** condition is unchanged.
- **Retained, not parked.** The state still satisfies G-13 cleanly; V-008 remains executable and is
  **amended** with a regime gate, a cost-floor arm and separate percentile and raw-level arms; **G-37** is
  opened on the within-contract regime-transfer question. The register's plain summary: the candidate's live
  content has narrowed to a *time-series* form of a *published* effect whose out-of-sample, cost-adjusted
  magnitude is not established and whose sign has been observed to invert across regimes.

#### Cycle 113 block — IDEA-009 · G-37 partially answered, and the answer to its third limb is adverse to the candidate's own validation plan

- **What was executed.** The Cycle-112 gap **G-37**, on its pre-registered route ("the sources behind S-389 and
  S-390, read for their regime definitions and their per-year signs, and any published 2025–2026 work on
  funding-rate regime classification"). S-389's source document was **re-fetched in full** — the Cycle-112
  entry had read it "from the abstract through Section 14", and the retrieval tool had truncated the final
  ~9 KB, which had therefore never been read — and read to the end (Section 15, Limitations; Section 16,
  Conclusion). The ledger entry is **upgraded** accordingly.
- **Limb (ii) — which regime is current: ANSWERED, and it is continuation, not reversal.** 2020–2023 against
  2024–2026: one hour **+0.33 % / +4.10 %**, two hours **−0.30 % / +3.61 %**, four hours **−0.30 % / +3.05 %**,
  eight hours **−0.29 % / +2.93 %**, twenty-four hours **−1.29 % / +2.39 %**; the 2020–2023 window is "59 % of
  the sample" and has "no effect at any horizon except a significantly negative one at a day (t = −3.0)",
  while 2024–2026 is significant at every horizon (twenty-four hours, t = 8.8). **The candidate's own
  construction flips with it**: the 90-day z-score's yearly 24-hour information coefficient runs **−2.3, −3.2,
  −0.3, +0.8, +1.1, +2.1, +0.5 %** (t = −1.5, −5.1, −0.4, +1.4, +2.4, +4.5, **+0.8**), so **in the current
  year the candidate's own state is statistically indistinguishable from zero** — not weakly negative, not
  negative.
- **Limb (iii) — can the flip be detected before a trade is taken: ANSWERED, AND ADVERSELY.** The regime *is*
  partly predictable ex ante: "Regressing each day's coefficient on its own trailing average, with a further
  day of embargo so that no trailing observation overlaps the outcome, gives a slope of **+0.65 to +0.78**
  across 63, 126 and 252-day windows at **t = 5.7 to 6.6**, and the trailing sign matches the next day's
  **54.7 % to 56.3 %** of the time. **But**: "**Knowing the regime is not the same as profiting from it:** a
  strategy trading the sign of its own trailing coefficient earns **+1.9 to +2.8 bps per day** more than always
  shorting high funding, but at **t = 0.64 to 0.88** and a block-bootstrap **p of 0.19 to 0.29** that gain is
  **not distinguishable from zero**." Read against the Cycle-112 amendment, which required the rule to be armed
  only when the *within-contract* trailing sign is negative, this is the nearest published measurement of
  exactly that gate: it is accurate only **barely above a coin flip**, and worth **nothing above noise** when
  traded.
- **Limb (i) — the within-contract, time-series form: STILL OPEN.** A full-text search of the retrieved
  document finds exactly **one** occurrence of the phrase "time-series form", and it concerns **cross-venue
  dispersion** — "The time-series form fares no better: market-wide disagreement carries a correlation of
  +0.02 (t = 1.0) with the next day's return" — not the funding level. The document's regime series is a
  **cross-sectional** daily information coefficient rolled over time. The transfer question G-37 asks is
  therefore **not** answered by the one document that would have answered it.
- **No score change (42.0), and the reason is recorded rather than assumed.** The Cycle-113 pre-registration
  registered **no score-move rule for Priority 3**, and the finding bears on the *gate*, not on the
  candidate's premise: resilience is already at the cell floor of **1**, and falsifiability was already
  lowered to **2** at Cycle 112 for the same class of reason (pre-answered predictions). A cell moves only on a
  pre-registered falsification rule with a defined bar, and the Cycle-52/66/112 precedents are applications of
  that discipline rather than exceptions to it.
- **A third hard-stop condition, standing and independent of the score: the regime gate cannot carry the
  rule.** A rule armed only when a trailing sign flips is, on the nearest published measurement, accurate at
  54.7 %–56.3 % and worth +1.9 to +2.8 bps per day at t = 0.64 to 0.88 (p = 0.19 to 0.29); any specification
  that depends on the gate must therefore carry its value from something other than the gate, and a
  specification that depends on the gate alone is a null by construction.
- **V-008 is amended, not re-created:** a fourth requirement, **(iv) a gate-value arm** — the gate's own
  trailing-sign match rate and the gate's own excess over the round trip must be reported **separately** from
  the rule's, and a gate that cannot be distinguished from zero must be reported as a **null on the gated
  rule**, not as a smaller edge. The specification remains **executable**.
- **G-37's status: open, narrowed to one limb.** Limbs (ii) and (iii) are answered and limb (iii)'s answer is
  adverse; limb (i) has no published test located, and the document that would most plausibly have supplied one
  does not contain it. **No new gap is opened** and the gap is not closed.

## Rank 1 — IDEA-011 ACCTCLOCK (69.0) — *new in Cycle 4; Cycle-13 rank 1; Cycle 16: 69.0, rank 1, down from 72.0; heading corrected in Cycle 75, which had still shown the Cycle-13 score; rubric profile corrected in Cycle 85, which still showed resilience 3/5 and data 4/5*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The accounting-standards adoption channel is
  well documented (Armstrong et al. 2010; Beuselinck et al. 2009), and the adoption dates are
  regulator-published years in advance — a clean, ex-ante knowable clock. Not 5 because the
  *two-legged trade specification* (drift from announcement to first full report, partial reversal)
  is conjectural rather than theory.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** Armstrong et al. (2010) and Beuselinck
  et al. (2009) are peer-reviewed and support the information-quality and enforcement-regime
  findings; the joint trading specification has no supporting empirical work located. Not 4
  because the candidate's specific claim (tradeable drift/reversal) is untested.
- **Substantive novelty (3/5):** The accounting-adoption effect family is documented; what is
  claimed is new: the dated, two-legged trade keyed to the adoption calendar and a pre-registered
  information-quality state. Modest but defensible novelty.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (4/5):** The predictions are
  sharp (information-quality interaction, enforcement-regime interaction, partial reversal,
  convergence benefit for high-quality firms), the state is observable from public filings, and
  the adoption calendar is public years in advance. Not 5 because the sample of adoption events is
  small (a handful of jurisdictions) and concurrent macro shocks are a severe confound.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (2/5, down from 3/5 in Cycle 16 — *bullet value corrected in
  Cycle 85, which still showed 3/5 while the maintained table read 3 → 9.0*):** The affected firms may be
  small-cap and less liquid; the holding horizon is quarters so financing accumulates; borrow on short
  legs can be scarce in small-cap names. Capacity is bounded by the number of adoption events. **The
  Cycle-16 fall is the enforcement-regime concentration of the published effect (S-178, S-179, S-221):
  the tradable drift is narrower across regimes than inside them, and the spread is crossed twice
  (entry and exit) in exactly the small-cap, low-information-quality names the long leg wants.**
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (5/5 — *bullet value corrected in Cycle 85, which still
  showed 4/5 after the Cycle-76 alignment to a mis-edited table cell; the original 5/5 stands*):** All
  inputs are public — regulator adoption calendars, firm-level returns, accounting-quality proxies,
  analyst following, country enforcement indices — with a known adoption calendar, and no proprietary
  input is required. The candidate's *state* is an assembly of published parts rather than a published
  aggregate, which is a **simplicity and construction** caveat, not an **availability** one; the only
  prospective input that is not yet published at all is the IAS 8.30–31 disclosure behind the killed
  arm A (the G-33 read found **0 of 6** interim reports quantifying it, re-check dated 2027 Q3), and that
  arm is dead on the kill condition, not on availability. **Availability, simplicity and reproducibility
  are therefore at the ceiling.**

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If the sample of adoption events is too small
for clean inference, the candidate is untestable as specified. If concurrent macro shocks explain
the entire drift, the candidate collapses into a known baseline. If the drift does not reverse
within 1–4 quarters, the candidate's payoff structure is wrong.

**Role in the portfolio.** The Cycle-4 *discovery* candidate with the highest data score (10/10)
in the register: a fully public state, a regulator-published clock, and a mechanism family not
previously in the register. The small event sample is the binding constraint.

---

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS (comfortably), G-12 PASS on the existence limb; no score change.**
The adoption calendars are regulator-published years in advance and the state — pre-adoption
information quality, enforcement regime, analyst following, foreign ownership — is computed from
filings that existed *before* the announcement, so the decision date, the event date and the state
are all fixed and public long before any position is taken. There is no
publication-versus-decision ordering to check, and this is the register's oldest and best-posed
G-13 case. G-12 passes on the existence limb: prediction (4) says high pre-adoption quality firms
show a smaller, *possibly negative*, drift, so the *existence* of the drift — and possibly its
sign — is conditioned on the state, while the published literature (Armstrong et al. 2010;
Beuselinck et al. 2009) claims the information-content effect and not a tradable
announcement-to-first-report drift, so the candidate is not a re-description of published work.
The unchanged binding constraint is the **adoption-event-sample-small condition**: the number of
independent adoption events is small and the cross-section within a jurisdiction is not
independent. That — not the state, not the timing, not the originality — is what limits this
candidate.

**Cycle-13 re-check — G-13 PASS, G-12 PASS on the existence limb; no score change; rank 3 → 1.**
Nothing found in Cycle 13 bears on this candidate: the screens are untouched, and no new evidence
was located about the accounting-adoption drift. The rank change is entirely an artefact of
IDEA-001's rejection (R-011) and IDEA-004's re-score (74.0 → 69.0). The candidate is now the
register's rank-1 item **with an unchanged evidence base and an unchanged hard-stop flag**, which
makes it the candidate whose next step is least specified. Two observations are recorded for the
next cycle. First, the *mirror image* of the check that killed IDEA-001 has never been run for this
candidate: the literature of record (Armstrong et al. 2010; Beuselinck et al. 2009) is largely
pre-2015, and nobody has checked whether the announcement-to-first-report drift survives the
post-2010 sample — if the drift has decayed the way the momentum factor did (S-156), the candidate
is a historical curiosity, and if it has not, that is a materially stronger evidence base than the
3/5 currently scored. Second, the binding constraint is sample size, so the *only* way to improve
  the candidate's prospects is to widen the event set (more jurisdictions, more adoption types, or a
  pooled cross-jurisdiction design) — which is a specification question, not a literature question.

#### Cycle 113 block — IDEA-011 · the Priority-2 post-2010 re-read returns a null on the score; a third hard-stop condition; a mandatory control; a new dated monitor

- **What was executed.** The **rediscovery guard** applied to the register's only unblocked candidate: a
  bounded post-2010 re-read of its nearest published test, on the three pre-registered routes (2025–2026 work
  on the IFRS-18 adoption event; citations to and extensions of S-253; 2026 evidence on the adoption-event
  information environment), with the score rule fixed in advance — **a published null or a sign reversal
  moves the score under the Cycle-66 precedent; a source supporting only the mechanism does not**.
- **What was found.** **No published null and no sign reversal.** **S-406** (Cui, Lin & Yang 2026,
  *Accounting Horizons*, DOI-bearing and peer-reviewed) is the nearest published post-2010 test of the
  candidate's mechanism and finds "**modest** improvements" that are "**statistically significant, although
  economically small**" — a **magnitude** finding, and under the register's G-12 ordering a magnitude finding
  is neither a null nor a sign reversal, so **no cell moves**. Two constraints on how far S-406 can bear are
  recorded: its design is a **reclassification of historical GAAP data** and so is not post-implementation
  market-effect evidence, and it tests the *informativeness of the subtotals*, not a post-adoption *drift*.
  **S-407** (De Melo Gaspar Neto 2025, Harvard Dataverse) reports that the effect of the same standard is
  "**65 %** mediated by the quality of institutional enforcement" and is "**73 % larger**" where enforcement is
  strong. **S-408** (GECAT 2026, Harvard Dataverse) is a **protocol-level** systematic-review frame for the
  sibling IFRS 15 literature — a reusable route, not evidence, and the register makes no claim from it about
  IFRS 18. The adverse premise reading falls on the **already-registered S-267** and is recorded there.
- **The finding that matters most for this candidate.** A first-adopter jurisdiction and a repeat-adopter
  jurisdiction differ in **enforcement quality** as well as in the candidate's conditioner, so the G-21
  comparator is **confounded unless enforcement is controlled for**. This is registered as a **V-009 control
  requirement** — mandatory, not optional — and it is *not* a score move: it changes the **interpretation** of
  the comparator, not the existence of the premise, and under G-12 a conditioner that changes size is a
  control question rather than a falsification.
- **The adverse premise reading, and why it moves nothing.** ESMA's public statement (S-267) requires issuers
  to disclose "the expected material effects from the application of IFRS 18 in the interim and annual
  financial reports for periods ending before 1 January 2027" — a mandate that publishes the *structure* of the
  accounting break **before** the event, which pre-empts the slow-diffusion premise. The mitigation is in the
  same source: the *mandated* content is **qualitative** (a description of the changes in the structure of the
  statement of profit or loss, the assessment of which activities are "specified main business activities",
  and the MPM definitions) while the *quantified* expected operating-profit change is only **encouraged**. The
  pre-emption is therefore **partial and structural, not quantitative**, and it weakens the premise in the
  *size* dimension, not the sign/existence dimension — by the direct precedent of the Cycle-108 S-387 finding,
  an adverse premise finding that moved no cell.
- **A third hard-stop condition, standing and independent of the score: the slow-diffusion premise must
  survive the ESMA-mandated pre-adoption disclosure.** If the market can anticipate the *sign* of the
  comparability surprise before the event, the premise is consumed and the candidate's edge is a
  re-description of a known disclosure rather than an information-diffusion event. The condition is urgent
  because of S-406: if the accounting break is worth only "modest" and "economically small" amounts of
  information, there is very little to diffuse slowly.
- **V-009 is amended, not re-created:** a **competing-conditioner control** (institutional enforcement
  quality) and a **pre-emption reporting arm** (the **2026 interim reporting season**, periods ending
  **30 June 2026**, per S-267's timing note), in which a **significant pre-event drift is reported as evidence
  that the premise has already been consumed — a kill signal, not a null on the effect.** The hypothesis, the
  arms, the cost model and the kill criteria are unchanged, and the specification remains **executable**.
- **A new dated monitor, and the earliest observation date in this dossier.** The first ESMA-expected IFRS 18
  pre-disclosure cycle lands in the **2026 interim reporting season** — earlier than the **2027 Q3** G-32 limb
  (c) / G-33 re-check and earlier than the 1 January 2027 effective date.
- **Effect. No score change (69.0, rank 1 unchanged); no gap opened or closed** (G-21 remains unrun by
  design — the comparator is a **validation** task, not a literature task); **no `IDEA-` identifier
  consumed**; **no `V-` identifier consumed**; three `S-` identifiers consumed (S-406, S-407, S-408).

---


## Withdrawn — IDEA-012 CLS-CLOSE (56.0) — *rejected (R-010) in Cycle 5; not a candidate*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The settlement-side deadline mechanics are
  documented by CLS (S-048, S-049) and the benchmark administrator (S-045); settlement risk is a
  priced friction (S-082, S-071). Not 5 because the *specific deadline-cluster price effect* is
  untested (G-11).
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (2/5):** S-082 (IMF WP) and S-071 (JFE) show
  settlement-side mechanics matter for pricing but do not test the CLS deadline cluster or the
  post-US-close T+1 window. No located paper tests IDEA-012's specific claim. Not 3 or above
  because the evidence base for the candidate's specific object is absent.
- **Substantive novelty (3/5):** The fix-window effect is published (rejected as R-007); what is
  claimed is new: the settlement-side residual beyond the fix. Modest novelty, but contingent on
  G-11 remaining open.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (3/5):** The predictions are
  sharp (signed move in funding direction, absent pre-T+1, absent in placebo, cost floor), but the
  test requires intraday order-book data that is not public (the fix literature used
  administrator-provided tick data). Not 4 because data access is the binding constraint.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (2/5):** The concession is measured in pips inside a few
  minutes; spread and slippage are first-order. This is the weakest resilience score in the
  register alongside IDEA-006.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (2/5):** The CLS timeline and benchmark calendar
  are public; intraday order-book or print data for the major pairs is not. The binding constraint.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** If G-11 closes against the candidate (a
published paper tests the settlement-side deadline cluster as a price determinant), the candidate
is rejected, not re-scored. If intraday data of adequate quality is unavailable, the candidate is
untestable as specified. If the concession is below round-trip cost in every pair, the candidate
is dead.

**Role in the portfolio.** A dedicated vehicle for the settlement-side residual of IDEA-006,
blocked on G-11. Its value was as a literature check: if the settlement-side cluster is tested and
found priced, IDEA-006 and IDEA-012 are both rejected; if untested, they remain as narrow,
unproven residuals. **Outcome: the check was run and it returned "tested and found priced" — a
settlement deadline cluster is a published price determinant (S-112), the securities settlement
cycle is a published price-impact determinant (S-113, S-114, S-115), and the post-US-close FX
window is documented as a cost rather than an edge (S-117 … S-120). Both candidates were rejected
(R-009, R-010). The market-structure facts established by the closure are kept as baselines, because
they are useful as *costs* in the design of any future candidate that must trade in or around that
window.**

---

## Parked — IDEA-013 EU-ETSCOMP (58.0) — *new in Cycle 5; parked (P-008) in Cycle 5*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (4/5):** The statutory obligation and its
  price-inelasticity are documented (S-102, S-103) and the effect is published (S-101). Not 5
  because the *candidate's own* conditional structure is not part of the published work and the
  published sample contains a high-surplus year in which the effect persisted.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (3/5):** The obligation, penalty, calendar and
  the aggregate state are all primary regulatory sources (S-102 … S-109, S-121). Not 4 because the
  state that the candidate conditions on is published *after* the pre-2023 deadline (G-13) and
  because the published model's own construction points at operator-level, not aggregate, balances.
- **Substantive novelty (2/5):** The surrender-month premium is published (S-101) and the published
  model already implies the state-conditional price structure; what is claimed is an *empirical*
  conditional structure and a post-2023 regime that the published sample does not cover.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (2/5):** The predictions are
  sharp and the kill conditions are explicit, but the decision-time form has **three observations**
  and none in a deficit year, and the published sample's own persistence statement cuts against the
  sign-flip prediction.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, regime (3/5):** Thin market, large policy-event risk, and a
  one-observation-per-year design in which a single position can move the price it predicts.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (3/5):** The state, the calendar and the auction
  data are public; the futures price series is venue-priced and the registry files are bulk. The
  binding constraint is the number of independent observations, not access.

**Why parked rather than retained or rejected.** The required full-text check on the published work
was performed and the candidate is *not* a re-description of published work — but three findings
make it untestable now: the published model implies the conditional structure; the published sample
contains a high-surplus year in which the effect persisted; and the aggregate state was published
*after* the pre-2023 deadline it was meant to inform, leaving three decision-time observations and
no deficit year. Re-specifying around operator-level balances would be a disguised baseline. The
unblocking event is dated: see P-008.

---

## Rank 3 — IDEA-014 RIN-DEADLINE (52.0) — *Cycle 112: rank 3 (only because IDEA-009 fell past it)* — *new in Cycle 6; Cycle-13 rank 6; Cycle 32: 52.0, rank 5, down from 60.0; heading corrected in Cycle 75, which had still shown the Cycle-13 score and rank*

- **Mechanism, causal coherence, persistence (3/5, down from 4/5 in Cycle 58 — *bullet value corrected in
  Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-58 value*):** The chain — a price-insensitive, statutory,
  date-bound obligation on a credit with a published price — is explicit, and each link is
  documented by a different body of work: the mechanism template is published in another venue (the
  EU ETS surrender-month premium, S-101), the RIN price and its pass-through are peer reviewed
  (S-134, S-135), the obligation's size is codified (40 CFR 80.1405 — S-128), the deadline is
  published by regulation (40 CFR 80.1451(f)(1)(iii) — S-129), and the deadline's movement is
  published rule (S-131). Not 5 because the mechanism is borrowed from another venue and the
  *differentiated* link — that the sign of the deadline effect is determined by the published net
  obligated position — is a conjecture that no located work tests. **Cycle 58 reduced this to 3**
  because that differentiated link has a **broken first link**: the projected position is calibrated
  to zero, and where a projected shortfall does occur the obligation is **waived rather than bought** —
  the deadline-cluster *existence* claim is untouched, the *state-determined sign* link is not.
- **Quality and independence of supporting evidence (2/5):** The components are well sourced
  (a primary regulatory text, two Federal Register rules, a regulator price series, a venue
  specification, and two peer-reviewed articles), but **the effect itself is unevidenced**: no
  located work states the deadline effect with the sign determined by the published net position.
  The published market-quality evidence is also *against* the candidate (S-133). The 2 reflects an
  evidence base for the mechanism and none for the claim.
- **Substantive novelty (3/5):** The EU ETS instance of this template is published (S-101) and is
  a baseline; within the US RIN market the **state-conditioned, two-sided** deadline claim is
  unclaimed. Modest but defensible novelty — and the honest kind: the candidate is not presented as
  a new mechanism, only as a new instance with a state that decides its sign.
- **Clarity of falsifiable predictions and feasibility of a clean test (2/5, down from 3/5 in Cycle 58 —
  *bullet value corrected in Cycle 76, which still showed the pre-Cycle-58 value*):** The predictions
  are sharp and two-sided (prediction 1 is falsifiable in both directions; predictions 2 and 3 add a
  vintage cross-section and a magnitude link), and the state inputs are all published. Not 4
  because the time-series sample is small (roughly fifteen annual deadlines, and the deadline has
  already moved twice in 2026), the published price series is a lagged weekly volume-weighted
  average with published filters and truncation (S-132), and the generation data's lag leaves the
  final weeks of the compliance year unpublished before the deadline. **Cycle 58 reduced this to 2**
  because prediction 2 — "the published net obligated position determines the sign" — is now known
  to be **untestable as stated**, not merely untested: the projected position does not determine the
  sign, and its only signed component carried a **+78 % error** in the one year in which it has been
  measured (S-324). One of three predictions is dead as written.
- **Resilience to costs, capacity, financing, regime (2/5):** The published market-quality evidence
  runs directly against the candidate: roughly one-tenth the liquidity of comparable commodity
  futures and transaction costs peaking at five times theirs (S-133). The venue exists and is
  physically delivered into EPA's own moderation system (S-136), but no public liquidity statistic
  exists for it. Capacity is bounded by the size of the obligated party's net requirement, and the
  trade is short a credit with a documented squeeze history (2013) in exactly the states where the
  forced buying is strongest.
- **Data availability, simplicity, reproducibility (4/5):** Every input is published — the
  standards (S-128), the deadline (S-129), the annual rules (S-130), the deadline extensions
  (S-131), the generation data, the weekly price series (S-132) and the futures venue (S-136).
  Not 5 because the published price series is not an executable quote, the price series' own
  filters truncate it, and the state is an *assembly* of published parts rather than a published
  aggregate.

**Cycle-6 re-check — G-13 PASS (the candidate's defining property), G-12 PASS on the sign limb;
new candidate at 60.0, status `candidate`.** Three independent regulatory facts carry G-13: the
obligation's **size** is codified in the CFR for two future years (40 CFR 80.1405: 2026 total
renewable fuel standard 15.50%, 2027 15.78%; amended 91 FR 16487, 1 April 2026 — S-128); the
**deadline** is not merely knowable but *published by regulation* — "The annual compliance
reporting deadline will be calculated in accordance with paragraph (f)(1)(i) of this section and
published on EPA's website" (S-129); and where the deadline has moved it moved **by published
rule** (the 2025 deadline moved from 1 September 2026 to 1 October 2026, with the next deadline
stated as 31 March 2027 — S-131). This is the candidate IDEA-013 could not be: **the state
precedes the decision**. G-12 passes on the sign limb: the claim is two-sided — a net-buyer state
produces a rise into the deadline and a fall after it, a net-seller state the reverse — so the
published state determines the sign of the trade and the claim is falsifiable in both directions;
the published EU ETS instance (S-101) has no such state and is therefore not a re-description of
it. **The sign is not asserted anywhere**: it is the candidate's first empirical test (gap **G-15**),
which is why it enters at 60.0 and not higher, and no validation specification is written in
Cycle 6.

**Key uncertainties that could change the rank.** The published market-quality evidence is the
most likely kill (S-133). The second is the squeeze risk on the short side in the net-short
states. The third is the small time-series sample: roughly fifteen annual deadlines, of which the
two most recent have been moved by rule, so the effective count of clean observations is smaller
than the calendar suggests. The fourth is model risk in the state itself — the net obligated
position is an assembly of published standards, published generation, carryover and exemption
decisions, and an error in that assembly flips the sign of the trade.

**Role in the portfolio.** The Cycle-6 *frontier-family* candidate: the only survivor of the five
mechanism families the Cycle-5 handoff named, and the register's first candidate in a **statutory
regulatory-credit** venue. It is also the register's first candidate whose *decision calendar* is
itself a published regulatory quantity that can move — which is a feature for the G-13 test and a
risk for the backtest.

---

#### Cycle 112 block — IDEA-014 · two agency documents on two unused route forms; no score change; V-012 written

- **What the screen asked.** Whether **published** evidence exists that the RFS **compliance deadline**
  moves **RIN prices**, using route forms the register had never used on this candidate: the **agency's own
  impact analyses** and the **legislative-audit literature**, plus the academic RIN-price literature.
- **What was found.** **S-393** (GAO-23-105801, November 2022) and **S-394** (EPA-420-R-22-038, December
  2022). They establish that **the obligation is priced** — GAO's own analysis of EPA's EMTS transaction data
  finds the smaller obligated party paying or receiving prices "**2.4 percent higher or lower** … on average"
  and "**2.9 percent**" between refineries, "statistically significant", with EPA's final analysis putting
  the 24 small refineries at "**1.1 % (1.2 ¢) more per RIN**" over the daily average and D4 at **2.7 %**,
  against EPA's stated sensitivity band of **5.3 % less to 7.5 % more** — and that the **deadline's timing
  structure** is as the candidate assumes (compliance dates "generally March 31 of the year following the
  compliance year"; current- or prior-year vintages both usable; exemption decisions issued after the
  90-day deadline **89 %** of the time, so refineries "often do not know whether they need RINs until close
  to, or after, the March 31 compliance deadline"). They **do not** establish a deadline-specific price
  effect: the documented differentials are **counterparty-size** effects, not **deadline-timing** effects.
- **Score: unchanged (52.0).** The Cycle-35/52 precedent applies — the sources bear on the mechanism and
  the cost floor, not on the conditional directional claim — so no cell moves. The **state-free existence
  limb remains inconclusive**: neither a positive finding nor a refutation.
- **A published friction anchor now exists, and it is hard.** The observable dispersion of RIN transaction
  prices around the daily average (1.1 % central per EPA; 2.4–2.9 % per GAO) is the only published,
  agency-audited measure of price friction around the RIN market, and it sets the order of magnitude any
  deadline effect must exceed to be tradeable. It is **not** a bid-ask spread, and the register does not treat
  it as one: **G-38** is opened for a published round-trip cost.
- **V-012 written — the candidate's first *state-free* specification.** The register's Cycle-6 rule is
  **amended, narrowly and on the record**: a *state-free* specification is now permitted, because G-15 is
  closed on the published-literature side, the candidate's only live claim is the state-free existence test,
  that test requires no signed state by construction, and the two new entries supply the public
  transaction-price dataset and the published friction anchor the Cycle-7 search recorded as unavailable.
  **The bar on any state-conditioned specification is re-affirmed in full** — the conditional version stays
  retired with its hard stop and no specification may be written for it.
- **Rank: 4 → 3, and only because IDEA-009 fell past it.** IDEA-014's own standing is unchanged, and the
  register records that plainly rather than presenting the rank move as an improvement.

#### Cycle 113 block — IDEA-014 · G-38 is closed, and the closure is adverse to the candidate's required effect

- **What was executed.** **G-38** — "the published round-trip cost of a RIN trade" — on its two pre-registered
  route forms, and it is **closed** on two published cost anchors: **S-404** (NERA Economic Consulting,
  "Ethanol RIN Market Analysis and Potential Reforms", 18 October 2018, filed in EPA docket
  EPA-HQ-OAR-2018-0775) and **S-405** (Lade, Lin & Smith, "Ex Post Costs and RIN Prices under the RFS",
  Resources for the Future Discussion Paper 15-22, June 2015). **Both are index-rendered** — the docket
  document was read through the search index and the RFF landing page and PDF refused a direct fetch (HTTP 404
  and HTTP 403 respectively) — and the first is a **consultancy paper prepared for a party with a commercial
  interest in the reform**, so the register relies on the **order of magnitude** and not on the point estimates.
- **What they say.** On the **Roll (1984)** model of the effective bid-ask spread, the RIN market's frictions
  are **several times** those of comparable energy futures: RIN spreads reach **up to five times** comparable
  energy markets' spreads, transaction costs are of the order of **five times**, market **liquidity is of the
  order of one-tenth**, and RIN **price volatility is five to ten times** that of the comparable energy
  markets. The academic anchor (S-405) points the same way, from an author with no commercial interest in the
  reform.
- **Why the closure is adverse, and why it is a closure rather than a null.** The register's own dispersion
  anchors are **small in percentage terms**: GAO's size-differential estimate of **2.4 %–2.9 %** (S-393) and
  EPA's small-party premium of **1.1 % (1.2 ¢) per RIN**, with EPA's own stated sensitivity band of **−5.3 %
  to +7.5 %** (S-394). A market whose round-trip cost is **several times** that of comparable energy futures
  therefore places a **few-percent price difference inside the cost band, not above it**. So G-38 closes, and
  what it closes with is a **tighter cost floor** than the dispersion anchors alone implied — a *material*
  change to the candidate's kill test even though it changes no score.
- **The state-free existence limb stays INCONCLUSIVE, and that is unchanged by the closure.** G-38 asked for
  the *cost*, not for the *effect*. No published source estimates a deadline-specific pre-/post-31-March RIN
  price pattern; the two cost anchors bear on what a detectable pattern would have to clear, not on whether
  one exists. Per the register's Cycle-52 rule — "an absence of evidence may not be upgraded to a refutation,
  and a positive finding may not be inferred from a mechanism finding" — the same holds here from a cost
  finding. **The limb is not closed and the candidate is not parked.**
- **V-012 is amended, not re-created.** The cost floor is restated against the **published RIN cost anchor**
  rather than against the dispersion anchors alone, and the pass bar is **tightened** to it: a pass must clear
  the published RIN market cost floor itself, so a result that clears the dispersion anchors but not the
  market-cost floor is a **price-discontinuity** result and **not a tradeable-return** result. **The bar on
  any state-conditioned specification for IDEA-014 remains re-affirmed in full.** No `V-` identifier is
  consumed.
- **Effect. No score change (52.0, rank 3 unchanged); G-38 closed; no new gap opened; no `IDEA-` identifier
  consumed; no `V-` identifier consumed; two `S-` identifiers consumed (S-404, S-405).** The candidate's
  **dated re-screen conditions are narrowed**: the "published RIN bid-ask or round-trip cost estimate" limb is
  **satisfied and closed**; the two remaining limbs are a published estimate of a pre-/post-31-March RIN price
  pattern, and the EPA's own publication of a transaction-price series long enough to span a moved deadline
  (the two moved 2026 deadlines, **S-131**, remain the natural test and are unchanged).

## Cross-cutting notes for the next cycle

1. **The state-proxy problem is largely a venue-selection problem.** IDEA-002 fails on the proxy
   and IDEA-007 worked on the same mechanism because the dollar funding state is *published*
   (S-052, S-054) — before it was rejected on the literature (R-008); IDEA-004's crowd state is
   published through filings with a statutory cadence but its *validity* is unproven (G-04), and
   IDEA-005 used the same idea in futures because the regulator publishes the passive positions
   there (S-030) — until Cycle 6 parked it on the evidence (P-009); IDEA-008 was believed to work
   because short interest and securities-lending fees are published by exchanges and lending
   agents, and **Cycle 6 corrected that belief** — the lenders do report loan rates, but to a
   regulator that is not publicly disseminating them until March 2029 (S-126), so the venue was the
   right idea on the wrong calendar (P-010); IDEA-009 works because the perpetual funding rate is
   published by the exchange every funding period with zero lag; IDEA-010 works partially —
   ETF flow is published daily but AP-level balance-sheet data is commercial (T6/G-01), and the FCA's
   own study of the same mechanism had to use a regulator's MiFID II database to see AP inventory
   (S-123); IDEA-011 works because adoption dates are regulator-published; IDEA-012 failed because
   intraday order-book data is not public (T5/G-08) and then because the effect was published
   (R-010); IDEA-013 works on publication but fails on *timing* (G-13); **IDEA-014 works because
   the obligation's size, the deadline and its revisions are all published by the regulator before
   the decision (S-128, S-129, S-131)**. The pattern is deterministic: *do not hunt for a proxy;
   hunt for a venue where the state is already published by a regulator, a benchmark administrator,
   a settlement utility, or the venue itself* — and, added in Cycle 6, *check the publication
   calendar of that state against the decision calendar before assuming the venue qualifies.* See
   the state-proxy methodological note at the end of `idea_register.md`.
2. **A published state is not automatically a decision-time state (new in Cycle 5, applied in
   Cycle 6).** Condition (e) of the state-proxy note is now a screening condition: the state's
   publication date must precede the decision date, per observation. The established instance is
   the EU ETS aggregate balance (S-121, G-13), which was published *after* the surrender deadline
   it was meant to inform until the 2023 compliance year. A second, softer instance is the T+1
   window's own market structure: the post-close liquidity picture is published (S-117, S-119,
   S-120), but it is published *as a cost*, not as a return. **Cycle 6 produced two further
   instances of opposite sign, and they are the reason the test earns its place.** On the negative
   side: the SEC's Rule 10c-1a loan-rate data — the state IDEA-008 names — are not publicly
   disseminated until **29 March 2029** (S-126), so a candidate can name a genuinely published
   quantity and still fail condition (e) on a calendar; and FINRA's short-interest file is
   published roughly seven to eight business days after its settlement date (S-127), which makes
   it a *stale* state rather than a decision-time one. On the positive side: the RFS obligation's
   size is codified in advance and its deadline is published by regulation, so IDEA-014 satisfies
   condition (e) in a way IDEA-013 could not.
3. **Several candidates are refinements of published effects, and three candidates have now been
   rejected outright.** IDEA-001 must be described as a refinement of a published effect, and the
   Cycle-6 G-12 screen says precisely *which part* of it survives: the side asymmetry and the
   second leg, not the flow scaling; IDEA-007 was a **narrow residual** of a published
   funding-constraint result (S-055) after the aggregate version was rejected as R-006 and the
   cross-section was rejected as R-008; IDEA-006 and IDEA-012 were **narrow residuals** of published
   fix-window and settlement-side effects after their aggregate versions were rejected as R-007 and
   then closed as R-009 and R-010; IDEA-008 and IDEA-009 are **new specifications** whose components
   are documented but whose joint two-legged expressions are not known baselines; IDEA-010, IDEA-011
   and IDEA-013 are likewise specifications whose components are documented but whose joint
   expressions are untested; and **IDEA-014 is a published mechanism template in an unclaimed
   instance** (the EU ETS surrender-month premium, S-101, applied to a different market with a
   different regulator and a state that decides the sign). If a future cycle adds a candidate whose
   only claim is "a known effect with a new name", it belongs in `rejected_and_parked.md`.
4. **The register has narrowed, and the narrowing was earned — twice.** After Cycle 13 the active
   ranking is **six** candidates: IDEA-011 (72.0), IDEA-003 (71.0), IDEA-004 (69.0), IDEA-010
   (65.0), IDEA-009 (60.0), IDEA-014 (60.0); **four** are parked (IDEA-002, IDEA-005, IDEA-008,
   IDEA-013) and **four** are rejected (IDEA-001, IDEA-006, IDEA-007, IDEA-012). **Cycle 30 changed
   the shape by one**: **five** are parked (IDEA-015 joins at 61.0, P-011) and the active ranking is
   unchanged at six, three of them shortlisted. The register holds
   **no FX candidate**, **no commodity-futures candidate**, **no settlement-window candidate** and,
   new in Cycle 13, **no index-reconstitution candidate**. The two standing screens are now doing
   real work rather than sitting as principles — Cycle 6's G-12/G-13 pass parked two candidates, and
   Cycle 13's application of them to a candidate whose *premises* had been quietly wrong (the
   effective-close convention, the passive-asset scale) killed the rank-1 candidate: **the register's
   most valuable single act was re-reading an existing candidate against newer literature, not
   generating a new one.** The next cycle's marginal value is therefore highest in (a) the same
   operation applied to the remaining candidates, in particular IDEA-011's post-2010 evidence base,
   and (b) new mechanism families in *venues not yet used* — the register now covers listed US
   equities, index options, crypto perpetuals, corporate-bond ETFs, US biofuel credits and
   accounting adoption. **Corrected in place in Cycle 87 (lossless).** This sentence originally read
   "and nothing in commodity futures, FX, fixed income cash, energy or weather", which was true of the
   register's *candidates* but is no longer true of its *screens*: commodity futures and FX were screened
   in **Cycle 2** (both candidates since rejected), fixed-income cash was screened in **Cycle 80 (R-048)**,
   and the weather-derivative family was screened in **Cycle 87 (R-049)**. The register still holds **no
   commodity-futures candidate, no FX candidate and no weather-derivative candidate**, so the standing
   instruction is unchanged: it is still a **venue** instruction, not another state variable in listed US
   equities. **Cycle 30 adds a family but not a venue**: IDEA-015 is listed US equities again, reached
   through corporate disclosure law rather than through a market operator, a regulator or an exchange,
   so the register's *venue* diversity is unchanged. The standing instruction to the next cycle is
   therefore still a **venue** instruction, not another state variable in listed US equities.
5. **Tail concentration is the register's main unhedged risk.** IDEA-003's payoff is concentrated in
   a few events; ~~IDEA-001's leg 2~~ is gone (the candidate was rejected in Cycle 13, R-011);
   IDEA-004's left tail is a
   broken anchor; IDEA-008's dominant failure mode is a recall driven by fundamental news (a
   directional loss, not a liquidity event); IDEA-009's worst case is a crypto deleveraging spike that
   gaps through the pre-scheduled exit; ~~IDEA-010's worst case is a credit stress event that widens
   spreads permanently~~ (**IDEA-010 was parked in Cycle 78, P-014 — so the register's active book now
   holds no fixed-income candidate at all, and the credit tail it warned about has left the book with it;
   the remaining tail concentration is IDEA-011 and IDEA-014's**); IDEA-011's worst case is a market-wide crash during the adoption window;
   **IDEA-014's worst case is a squeeze against a short position in the very states where the
   forced buying is strongest — the 2013 RIN episode is the documented precedent, and the
   deadline concentration multiplies it.** No single candidate is a complete portfolio, and none of
   them should be described as if it were.
6. **The register now has market diversification and factor concentration.** Cycle 1's candidates
   were all listed US equities; Cycle 2 added commodity futures and two FX candidates (both since
   rejected); Cycle 3 added securities-lending equities (IDEA-008, now parked) and crypto perpetuals
   (IDEA-009); Cycle 4 added corporate-bond ETFs (IDEA-010), accounting-adoption equities (IDEA-011)
   and FX settlement (IDEA-012, rejected); Cycle 5 added an EU-allowances candidate (IDEA-013,
   parked); **Cycle 6 added a US biofuel-credit candidate (IDEA-014) and, with IDEA-005 and
   IDEA-008 parked, removed the commodity-futures leg from the active book entirely.** The remaining
   book is therefore equity-heavy (three candidates: IDEA-003, IDEA-004, IDEA-011 — IDEA-001 was
   removed in Cycle 13), with
   credit (IDEA-010), crypto (IDEA-009) and a regulatory credit (IDEA-014) on the side. The
   register's net dollar, credit and crypto exposures are portfolio concerns that no single dossier
   owns.
7. **No candidate has been tested.** Nothing in this register is evidence that any of these
   hypotheses works, and no number here is a performance estimate.
8. **The originality gaps on the Cycle-2/3/4 candidates have been addressed as far as literature
   search can take them, and the Cycle-5, Cycle-6 and Cycle-13 passes closed eight of them.** G-10
   is **CLOSED against IDEA-007** (R-008) and G-11 is **CLOSED against IDEA-006 and IDEA-012**
   (R-009, R-010). G-06 (IDEA-005) is **closed as a bounded negative finding** with contradicting
   evidence now recorded (S-098, S-099): the published literature conditions spot–futures
   convergence on *trader type*, and the closest published test of IDEA-005's own state variable
   (CIT market share) reports no significant effect — **and in Cycle 6 that closed G-12 against the
   candidate as well, which parked it (P-009)**. G-09 (IDEA-006) is closed. **Cycle 6 applied G-12
   and G-13 as screens to every active candidate**: G-13 passed for IDEA-001, IDEA-003, IDEA-004
   (conditionally, on a filing-anchored rule), IDEA-009, IDEA-011 and IDEA-014, and **failed for
   IDEA-008** (S-126); G-12 passed for every retained candidate, but on IDEA-001 only on the
   *existence* limb. **Cycle 13 closed three more and opened one.** **G-02/G-07 are CLOSED as
   construction questions**: a published method for estimating benchmark-specific tracked assets now
   exists (S-148 infers the AUM tracking each of five named indices from reconstitution-day closing
   volume, 2000–2021), so the question is no longer "is this estimable?" but "is the underlying
   per-security auction history available?". **G-03 is CLOSED**: the effective-close convention is
   documented by the index manager itself (S-149) and by peer-reviewed work (S-150, S-148). **G-05's
   literature half is CLOSED against IDEA-003's single-name extension** (S-153, S-154) — the
   aggregate version is unaffected. **G-18 is NEW**: validate a return-correlation crowding proxy
   (S-157, comomentum) against a holdings-based crowding measure (S-026) — this is what would
   unblock IDEA-004's proxy-validity condition (G-04), and it must be closed *before* V-002 is
   executed. **Still open and worth the next cycle's attention: G-01** (a public dealer funding-state
   proxy — narrowed in Cycle 13 to a dealer-level FVA series, with the funding-state family itself
   now published, S-160–S-162), **G-04** (the crowd-depth proxy for IDEA-004 — now with a named
   candidate proxy and an open validation, G-18), **G-14** (a public borrow-cost series — dated, not
   before 29 March 2029), **G-15** (the RIN compliance-deadline price pattern — IDEA-014's first
   test), **G-16** (a validated public proxy for dealer balance-sheet stress — IDEA-010's
   precondition).

**Cycle-30 gap update.** **G-18 is RESTATED, not closed** — the newest peer-reviewed 13F-crowding
work (S-186) makes the holdings-based construction an active published object but associates
crowding with prices, comovement and fragility rather than with a return-predictive rule, and
nothing located in this cycle validates a contemporaneous proxy against it. **G-19 is NEW**
(IDEA-003): is there published work that states the *expected*-premium convention explicitly and
tests the crash-state sign of that quantity — S-180 extracts the premium from variance-swap quotes
with a horizon distinction (closest to the expected convention), S-181 uses the ex-post convention,
and the candidate's own quantity is stated by neither. **G-20 is NEW** (IDEA-014): is the
*projected* net obligated position a sufficient sign-determining state, or does projection error
swamp it — the projection has already been revised once, from the Set 2 Rule's projection to a 770
million RIN relief (S-184), and the realised position publishes only after the deadline it informs.
**G-21 is NEW** (IDEA-015): is there published work that constructs a security-level *remaining
scheduled supply* state from Item 408(a) and Form 4 filings and documents a price effect attached
to the *execution* window rather than the adoption window (S-182, S-183)? **G-15 is SHARPENED**
rather than closed: the RIN sign test must now be run on the *projected* net obligated position, with
the realised position as a post-hoc control only.
9. **Bounded tasks T5–T8 status (updated in Cycle 13).** T5 (G-03/G-08 closing-auction
   concentration): **CLOSED** — the convention question (G-03) is answered and documented (S-149,
   S-150, S-148), and the aggregate participation question is answerable from published venue
   statistics (S-100: NYSE auction statistics and Cboe's date-addressable market-share file with an
   "Exclude Auctions" control); what remains is only the long-history *per-security* auction-volume
   series, which is a commercial product, and the *attribution* question, which cannot be answered
   from aggregate volume at all. T6 (G-01 composite funding spec): SOFR (S-041) and
   standing-repo/primary-dealer data (S-042) verified as public ingredients, but **Cycle 13
   re-confirmed the bounded negative with a sharper target** — the construct is peer-reviewed
   (S-155) and the funding-state family is published (S-160–S-162, including the public TGCR − IORB
   measure), but no published continuous *dealer-level* FVA series exists and none is likely to;
   IDEA-002 stays parked. T7 (G-05 single-name side): **CLOSED on both halves** — the data question
   was closed in Cycle 2 (the single-name 0DTE series is constructible from public OCC data, S-100)
   and the literature question is closed in Cycle 13 **against** the single-name extension (S-153,
   S-154). T8 (G-07 benchmark-specific passive-asset series): **CLOSED as a construction question**
   — a published method and published estimates exist (S-148); the remaining obstacle is
   data engineering, and the only consumer of the gap (IDEA-001) was rejected in the same cycle.
   **None of T5–T8 remains open; G-18 is the only gap opened in Cycle 13**, and it is the one gap
   that could plausibly unblock a top-three candidate (IDEA-004).
10. **A published effect can be true and the register's version of it false, because the quantity is
    convention-dependent (new in Cycle 30).** IDEA-003's gate claims that in an imminent-crash state
    the variance risk premium "collapses" and short-volatility exposure becomes uncompensated. The
    2026 preprint that frames the premium as crash-conditional and "not harvestable carry" (S-181)
    is not a refutation of that claim — it measures the premium as implied minus *subsequently
    realized* volatility, which mechanically **widens** in stress because fear over-prices volatility
    that then mean-reverts, whereas the candidate's claim is about the **expected** premium. The two
    quantities can move in opposite directions in the same state, so a candidate whose prediction is
    stated in convention-free language is **untestable in principle**, and a null obtained under one
    convention does not kill a claim made under the other. This is now a standing requirement, added
    to the two existing screens: **every future dossier that conditions on a measured spread or
    premium must state the measurement convention ex ante, and every validation specification must
    fix it before the holdout is opened.** V-003 has been amended accordingly in Cycle 30.
11. **A new class of decision-time state: publication by corporate disclosure law (new in Cycle 30).**
    IDEA-015's state is published not by a market operator, a regulator or an exchange, but by
    **the issuer and the insider under securities law** — Item 408(a) quarterly tables in the
    10-Q/10-K, and Form 4 reports of individual plan sales with the plan's adoption date and,
    sometimes, its remaining capacity (S-182, S-187). Two features make this class attractive for
    future screening: the state is **mandatory and standardised** (no vendor discretion, no
    publication discretion) and its **cadence is statutory**. Two features make it unattractive: the
    state is **coarse** (aggregate maximum size and window, not an execution schedule) and the
    **reconciliation** of individual Form 4 sales to individual plans is not always disclosed, so the
    state must be constructed rather than read. A future cycle that wants a new *venue* should look
    for this class — mandatory, standardised, statutorily cadenced disclosure — rather than another
    published market series.
12. **A slow state can still carry a daily rule, if the identification is cross-sectional (new in
    Cycle 35).** IDEA-010's capital state refreshes only when an AP files — quarterly, with a 40–90 day
    lag (S-225, S-226) — which looked fatal against a 1–10 session rule. It is not. A state that is
    constant between filings is fully usable for a claim of the form *"the level* of the state governs
    *the size* of an event-driven move", provided the identification is a **cross-sectional contrast
    between units with different states** rather than a within-unit time-series reading of state
    changes. Three conditions follow, and they generalise to every candidate whose state is a slow
    institutional quantity (IDEA-004's 13F crowd state is the other live case). (i) **The state is a
    filter over a cross-section; the event is what times the trade.** The flow, not the state, is the
    trigger. (ii) **The within-unit time-series reading is demoted to an ablation** and must be
    reported with its own effective-sample count, because the number of state *changes* per unit is
    small. (iii) **The effective sample must be counted in units of (state observation, event) rather
    than in sessions**, and "untested" must remain a permitted verdict when that count is too small —
    a null in a sample of one episode is not a kill. The general lesson: **a quarterly state is not a
    quarterly trade.** The error is to assume that a conditioning variable's refresh frequency must
    equal the holding horizon, which confuses the *conditioning variable* with the *trigger*. The same
    correction retires the Cycle-34 amendment's conditional instruction to extend the horizon or park
    IDEA-010 if the cadence proved infeasible: the cadence is feasible, the horizon is preserved, and
    the price of the resolution is a small effective sample that must be reported rather than
    glossed.
13. **A decision-time state must satisfy three tests, and the Cycle-52 screen closed the third (new in
    Cycle 52).** Until Cycle 52 the register's state requirements were two: the state must be **published
    before the decision** (G-13) and it must **determine the sign or the existence** of the effect
    (G-12). The R-032 venue-halt screen added a third, from an independent body of evidence: a state that
    is **broadcast in real time cannot carry an edge even when it is published and even when it is
    sign-carrying in principle**, because it is a public record of the flow it is supposed to predict
    (the LULD price bands are republished to the public every 30 seconds, S-260, and the EU regime
    *mandates* publication of trading halts, S-261). The three tests are therefore: **(i) published before
    the decision; (ii) sign-determining rather than merely informative; (iii) not a public real-time
    by-product of the flow it is meant to predict.** Two of the register's own candidates show what each
    failure looks like. **IDEA-014 fails (ii)** — the sign-determining RIN state publishes only *after* the
    deadline, and the published pre-deadline proxy is set against the agency's own supply projection, so
    the projected net position is near zero by construction (S-184, S-201); the LULD bands fail **(iii)**,
    and they fail it while passing (i) comfortably. **G-32's identification limb is the register's first
    case where all three must be satisfied at once**, which is a further reason it is the right thing for
    the next cycle to work on.
14. **A null that is merely re-declared is worth nothing; a null that is re-derived from a check is
    worth a cycle (new in Cycle 52).** The register's standard, established by the Cycle-40 execution of
    G-29 and the Cycle-37 and Cycle-51 repairs, is that a gap's status must be **re-derived from a
    check**, not carried forward in prose. G-21 stood as a null for **fourteen cycles** (Cycles 38–51)
    without a single new source; re-declaring it each cycle would have produced a long, confident record
    of nothing. Cycle 52 instead ran the check the register had never run for this candidate — a
    **prior-art re-read of the rank-1 candidate against literature it had not yet seen** — and that check
    paid out three ways: it **sharpened the null** from "no study found" to "the comparator is standard
    in this field and has been run on other outcomes, but not on drift" (S-253, S-265); it **surfaced a
    published conditioner that competes with the candidate's own state** and therefore became a mandatory
    control (S-252, alongside S-221); and it **produced a dated, prospective route** — IFRS 18's 1 January
    2027 effective date and the published jurisdiction-profile calendar (S-254, S-255) — which opened
    **G-32** where fourteen cycles of null-maintenance had not. **The general lesson, and the standing
    instruction for the next cycle: a candidate that has produced no new evidence for a long time should
    be *re-read*, not re-declared.** A long null is a prompt to change the operation, not a reason to
    repeat it.

**Cycle-52 gap update.** **G-32 is NEW** (IDEA-011): the **prospective IFRS-18 adoption window**.
IFRS 18 is effective for annual reporting periods beginning on or after **1 January 2027** and creates a
comparability break by design (S-255), and the jurisdiction-level adoption-status calendar the G-21
discriminator needs is published in advance in the IFRS Foundation's jurisdiction profiles, with **170
jurisdictions** filterable by "required for domestic public companies" versus "permitted but not required"
(S-254). Three limbs: **(a)** the calendar limb — do the published profiles identify a group A (first-time
IFRS 18 adopters) and a group B (second-time adopters) with **non-overlapping, pre-registered** adoption
dates? This is a **reading** task and is closable now. **(b)** the identification limb — does the
candidate's **firm-level** conditioning retain identifying power *within a single standard*, i.e. is the
treatment not absorbed by the two published country-level conditioners (enforcement **S-221**, language
distance **S-252**)? **(c)** the pre-adoption-claim limb — does any 2024–2027 literature already claim the
IFRS 18 drift sign? (S-253 cannot answer it: IFRS 18 post-dates the review.) **Dated re-check:** re-read
the IFRS 18 pre-adoption literature **annually**, and re-run limb (a) **whenever the IFRS Foundation
updates its jurisdiction profiles**. **G-21's null is re-derived, not closed**, and is now stated as an
*outcome-specific* unrun comparison on a standard comparator rather than as an absence of search. **G-30 is
EXHAUSTED** (IDEA-010; no re-probe, no substituted proxy; V-010 remains non-executable). **G-28 and V-002
remain FROZEN** (IDEA-004; the null is re-derived and no published convergence-crowd *concentration*
measure was located). **IDEA-014's state-free existence limb remains INCONCLUSIVE** — neither a positive
finding nor a refutation. **R-032 is NEW**: the venue-halt / price-band / market-wide-circuit-breaker
family, a bounded negative with a dated re-screen condition.

15. **A published *conditional* form supersedes an unconditional candidate, and a null on the unconditional
    form is not a kill (new in Cycle 66).** Cycle 66's re-read of IDEA-009 produced the register's clearest
    instance of a failure mode that every remaining candidate should be checked for. The published evidence on
    the funding-extreme reversal is **conditional on a second variable** — the direction of the market's move
    (S-310: +1.88 % reversal on up days against −1.74 % *continuation* on down days) — while the candidate
    claimed the **unconditional** sign. Three consequences follow, and they generalise.
    (i) **A candidate's state can pass every availability test and still be wrong in sign**, because the
    published effect it trades is conditioned on something the candidate never conditioned on. G-12 asks
    whether the state determines the sign; after Cycle 66 it must also ask *of the published effect, not of
    the candidate's own claim*.
    (ii) **When the published conditional form is stronger than the candidate's form, the candidate's
    residual collapses to a formulation difference** and the register must reclassify it as an incremental
    variant, exactly as Cycle 30 did for IDEA-003.
    (iii) **A null on the unconditional form must not be a kill** — the same convention amendment the register
    wrote into V-003 in Cycle 30 now applies to V-008 in Cycle 66. A candidate whose unconditional form is a
    published baseline must be pre-registered so that its *conditional* form is the one tested, and so that a
    null on the published form is reported as a **replication** rather than a failure.
    The general instruction for the next cycle: **when re-reading a candidate, look for published work that
    conditions the candidate's effect on a second variable, not only for work that tests the candidate's
    state.** The second variable is usually the cheapest thing to add and the most damaging to omit.
16. **The state-kind screen programme is exhausted as a source of new candidates (new in Cycle 66).** Seven
    consecutive template screens — R-040 (fee or toll, template C) through R-046 (published clamp/cap band,
    template I) — each produced a bounded negative with the same five reasons: published and anticipated;
    fails G-12 on sign or existence; conditions magnitude rather than existence; small effective sample;
    competed away. **The marginal yield of an eighth template is very likely negative.** The screen slot
    should be spent on **evidence operations** — the re-read of an existing candidate against literature it has
    not yet seen — which is what Cycle 66 did, and which produced the cycle's only change to a candidate
    (IDEA-009, 60.0 → 53.0, plus an amended V-008 and a reclassification). A template screen remains the
    right tool only for a **genuinely new state kind**, materially different from templates A–I; it must pass
    the strict bar with **G-12 first** before any dossier is written, and it must be recorded with its dated
    re-screen condition.
17. **A published bound on a state is a modifier, not an edge (new in Cycle 66).** The Cycle-66 screen found a
    state kind the register had not tested: a **published clamp, cap, floor or band** — the parameter that
    bounds a continuously published state so that the state is uninformative over a published interval. Such a
    band is a **deterministic function of an already-published price**, so conditioning on it adds no
    information; it changes the *magnitude and tails* of a state rather than the sign of an effect; and its
    useful role is as a **filter** — exactly how the register now uses it in V-008's Cycle-66 amendment, and
    how the published literature uses it (S-317, S-318). The lesson generalises: **when a published state is
    bounded, the bound is a state modifier to be recorded and excluded, not a candidate.** A candidate that
    conditions on a bound must show that the bound changes the *sign or existence* of an effect and is not a
    deterministic function of an already-published quantity.

**Cycle-66 gap update.** **No gap was opened and no gap was closed.** **G-28** (IDEA-004) is maintained as a
frozen structural null and **V-002 stays frozen**; **G-21** (IDEA-011) is handed off to validation with no
re-search and **V-009 is not amended**; **G-30** (IDEA-010) is confirmed exhausted with no re-probe and
**V-010 remains non-executable**; **IDEA-014's state-free existence limb** remains inconclusive with no gap
opened and no effort spent without a new source; **G-32 limb (c)** and **G-33's re-check** remain future
obligations dated **2027 Q3**, with the G-33 sample read not to be re-run from scratch. Cycle 66's own new
question is **not a gap**: IDEA-009's residual is a **validation** question, carried by the amended V-008, and
the register's standing rule — *a candidate that has produced no new evidence for a long time should be
re-read, not re-declared* — was applied to it and paid out.

**Cycle-70 gap update.** **No gap was opened and no gap was closed.** **G-28** (IDEA-004) is maintained as a
frozen structural null and **V-002 stays frozen**; **G-21** (IDEA-011) is handed off to validation with no
re-search and **V-009 is not amended**; **G-30** (IDEA-010) is confirmed exhausted with no re-probe and

**Cycle-113 state-integrity note for the next cycle.** **(i) The header-update failure mode has now occurred a
sixth time, and this time it was *partial*, which is a new variant of it.** The Cycle-113 session was
interrupted after it had written the cycle's substantive findings into `source_ledger.md` (header already
"Cycles 1–113", eleven entries S-398 … S-408, and the Cycle-113 gap-verdict block) and into
`rejected_and_parked.md` (header already "Cycle 120 complete", R-062, baseline rows 95 and 96), but before it
had written the matching material into `idea_register.md`, `ranked_shortlist.md` and `validation_handoffs.md`,
whose headers all still read **"Cycle 112"** — and before it had written the Cycle-113 outcomes into
`cycle_notes.md` or rewritten the handoff. The artifact set was therefore internally inconsistent in the
opposite direction to the usual failure: two artifacts ahead, three behind, and the durable handoff still
carrying a Cycle-112 banner *and* a Cycle-112 next-cycle task list. **This cycle completed the repair**: the
three lagging artifacts were brought to "Cycle 120 complete", the cycle log gained its outcomes section, and
the handoff was rewritten for Cycle 114. The lesson recorded is the same one the register has recorded five
times, with one addition: **a cycle that writes a header must finish the artifact set in the same session, and
a partially-written cycle must be completed rather than resumed** — a half-updated set is harder to read than
a stale one, because each artifact looks authoritative.
**(ii) The Cycle-113 screen's framing correction is a first for the register and is recorded here so that it is
not re-invented.** The Cycle-112 handoff described its two Priority-1 families as "neither of which appears in
the known-baseline table and neither of which has ever been screened". That was **false** — one family was a
**licensed re-screen** of the register's own closed templates R-042 and R-043, and the other was **already a
registered source and a parked candidate's own conditioning state**. The screen was therefore run against the
**correct, weaker** question ("does this instance satisfy the dated re-screen condition, and if so does it pass
the strict bar?"), and the correction was recorded **before** the screen ran rather than being silently
adopted. **The register's rule should be extended:** a handoff that asserts a family is *unscreened* must
carry the evidence of that assertion, and a re-screen must be reported as **weaker** than a first-time screen.
**(iii) The rubric arithmetic was re-run from this file's own table and is clean**, and **no score, rank,
tie-break, hard-stop flag, gap or specification status changed** in Cycle 113.
